\left\|A\big[Q_{k}(v,\rho,\cdot)\big](w)\right\|_{L^{2}(w\in\mathbb{R})}\lesssim\left\|\Psi_{2}(w-w_{\ast})A\big[Q_{k}(v,\rho,\cdot)\big](w)\right\|_{L^{2}(w\in\mathbb{R})}+\frac{C_{\gamma}}{|k|}\varpi_{\varkappa_{k},w_{\ast}}(v+w_{\ast},\rho+w_{\ast}).

(3.55)

It follows from (
3.46
)-(
3.52
) and (
3.55
) that for sufficiently small
\gamma\in(0,1)
and suitable
C_{\gamma}\in(0,\infty)
,

\begin{split}&\left\|A\big[Q_{k}(v,\rho,\cdot)\big](w)\right\|_{L^{2}(w\in\mathbb{R})}\lesssim C_{\gamma}\varpi_{k,w_{\ast}}(v+w_{\ast},\rho+w_{\ast})/|k|+\gamma^{1/2}M\varpi_{\varkappa_{k},w_{\ast}}(v+w_{\ast},\rho+w_{\ast})/|k|,\end{split}

(3.56)

which implies that

M\lesssim C_{\gamma}+\gamma^{1/2}M.

(3.57)

Choosing
\gamma\in(0,1)
sufficiently small, we conclude that

M\lesssim 1.

(3.58)

The desired bounds (
3.39
) follow from (
3.58
) by noting that
Q_{k}(v,\rho,w)=Q_{k}(v,\rho,w)\Psi_{2}(w-w_{\ast})
. The bounds (
3.40
) follow from taking derivative in (
3.46
) and estimating the resulting expression. The bounds (
3.41
) then follow from (
3.32
)-(
3.34
), the equation (
3.44
), and the bounds (
3.39
). We omit the routine details.

∎

4.
Spectrum of the linearized operator

In this section we study the spectrum of
L_{k}
for
k\in\mathbb{Z}\backslash\{0\}
.
Our main result is the following characterization of the spectrum of
L_{k}
for
k\in\mathbb{Z}\backslash\{0\}
. The result is not new, see for example
[
5
]
and references therein for more discussions and other aspects of the operator
L_{k}
. The simple proof below is based on the argument in
[
9
]
.

Proposition 4.1
.

Assume that
k\in\mathbb{Z}\backslash\{0\}
. Recall the definition (
1.16
)-(
1.17
) for the space
X_{k}
. The spectrum of
L_{k}:X_{k}\to X_{k}
is
[0,b(0)]
. For
|k|=1
,
\lambda=0
is an embedded eigenvalue for
L_{k}
with the corresponding eigenfunction
\Omega^{\prime}(r),r\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
, and there are no other discrete eigenvalues. For
k\in\mathbb{Z}\backslash\{0\}
and
|k|\neq 1
, there are no discrete eigenvalues.

Remark 4.2
.

The discrete eigenvalue
\lambda=0
for
L_{k}
with
|k|=1
is connected to the translation symmetry of the background flow, and can be treated by re-centering the radial vorticity profile, see the orthogonality condition (
1.29
).

Proof.

We can assume without loss of generality
k\in\mathbb{Z}\cap[1,\infty)
. Suppose
g\in X_{k}
with
\|g\|_{X_{k}}=1
is an eigenfunction corresponding to the eigenvalue
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}
. Writing for
r\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
,

g(r)=\partial_{r}^{2}\varphi(r)+\frac{\partial_{r}\varphi(r)}{r}-k^{2}r^{-2}\varphi(r),

(4.1)

we obtain that for
r\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
,

\Big(\frac{U(r)}{r}-\lambda\Big)\big(\partial_{r}^{2}\varphi+\frac{\partial_{r}\varphi}{r}-k^{2}r^{-2}\varphi\big)-\frac{\Omega^{\prime}(r)}{r}\varphi=0.

(4.2)

Note that

\begin{split}\varphi(r)=-\int_{0}^{\infty}G_{k}(r,\rho)g(\rho)\,d\rho=\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{\rho}{2k}\min\Big\{\big(r/\rho\big)^{k},(\rho/r)^{k}\Big\}g(\rho)\,d\rho.\end{split}

(4.3)

It follows from (
1.10
) and (
4.3
),
\|g\|_{X_{k}}=1
and Cauchy-Schwarz inequality that for
r\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
,

|\varphi(r)|+|r\partial_{r}\varphi(r)|=O\Big(\frac{r\langle\log{r}\rangle}{\langle r\rangle^{2}}\Big).

(4.4)

Define for
r\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
,

h(r):=\frac{\varphi(r)}{r(\lambda-U(r)/r)}.

(4.5)

We distinguish several cases.

Case 1:
\lambda\not\in[0,b(0)]
.

Then from (
4.2
) and (
4.4
) we see that
h\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+})
and for
r\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
,

|h(r)|\lesssim_{\lambda}\frac{\langle\log{r}\rangle}{\langle r\rangle^{2}},\quad|h^{\prime}(r)|\lesssim_{\lambda}\frac{\langle\log{r}\rangle}{r\langle r\rangle^{2}}.

(4.6)

Direct calculations show

\begin{split}\varphi(r)&=r(\lambda-U(r)/r)h(r)=(\lambda r-U(r))h(r),\\
\frac{\varphi^{\prime}(r)}{r}&=\frac{\lambda-U^{\prime}(r)}{r}h(r)+\big(\lambda-\frac{U(r)}{r}\big)h^{\prime}(r),\\
\varphi^{\prime\prime}(r)&=-U^{\prime\prime}(r)h(r)+2(\lambda-U^{\prime}(r))h^{\prime}(r)+(\lambda r-U(r))h^{\prime\prime}(r).\end{split}

(4.7)

Thus from (
4.2
) we obtain that

\begin{split}&(\lambda r-U(r))h^{\prime\prime}(r)+\Big[2(\lambda-U^{\prime}(r))+\lambda-\frac{U(r)}{r}\Big]h^{\prime}(r)\\
&\qquad+\Big[-U^{\prime\prime}(r)+\frac{\lambda-U^{\prime}(r)}{r}-\frac{k^{2}(\lambda-U(r)/r)}{r}+\Omega^{\prime}(r)\Big]h(r)=0.\end{split}

(4.8)

Using the identity

U^{\prime}(r)+\frac{U(r)}{r}=\Omega(r),

(4.9)

we obtain that

U^{\prime\prime}(r)+\frac{U^{\prime}(r)}{r}-\frac{U(r)}{r^{2}}=\Omega^{\prime}(r).

(4.10)

We can use (
4.10
) to simplify (
4.8
) as

(\lambda r-U(r))h^{\prime\prime}(r)+\Big[2(\lambda-U^{\prime}(r))+(\lambda-U(r)/r)\Big]h^{\prime}(r)+\frac{1-k^{2}}{r}\big(\lambda-U(r)/r\big)h(r)=0,

(4.11)

or equivalently

\begin{split}&r(\lambda r-U(r))^{2}h^{\prime\prime}(r)+\Big[(\lambda r-U(r))^{2}+2r(\lambda r-U(r))(\lambda-U^{\prime}(r))\Big]h^{\prime}(r)\\
&\qquad+(1-k^{2})r(\lambda-U(r)/r)^{2}h(r)=0.\end{split}

(4.12)

(
4.12
) can be reformulated as

\frac{d}{dr}\Big[r(\lambda r-U(r))^{2}h^{\prime}(r)\Big]+(1-k^{2})r(\lambda-U(r)/r)^{2}h(r)=0.

(4.13)

Multiplying
\overline{h}
and integrating over
(0,\infty)
, using also (
4.6
) to treat the boundary terms, we obtain that

\int_{0}^{\infty}r(\lambda r-U(r))^{2}|h^{\prime}(r)|^{2}+(k^{2}-1)r(\lambda-U(r)/r)^{2}|h(r)|^{2}\,dr=0.

(4.14)

Therefore,
h\equiv 0
if
k\neq 1
, a contradiction with the assumption that
\|g\|_{X_{k}}=1
. (
4.14
) also implies that
h\equiv C
for some nonzero constant
C
if
k=1
, and thus
\varphi(r)=C(\lambda r-U)
. Consequently from (
4.4
) we have
\lambda=0
, a contradiction with
\lambda\not\in[0,b(0)]
. In summary, we conclude that if
\lambda\not\in[0,b(0)]
, then
\lambda
cannot be a discrete eigenvalue of
L_{k}
.

Case 2:
\lambda\in(0,b(0))
.
We can assume that
\lambda=b(r_{0})=U(r_{0})/r_{0}
for some
r_{0}\in(0,\infty)
. From (
4.2
) and (
1.10
), it follows that
\varphi(r_{0})=0
. Using (
4.4
) and (
4.2
), we see also that
\varphi\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+})
. It follows that
h\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+})
and the following bounds hold for
r\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
,

|h(r)|\lesssim_{\lambda}\frac{\langle\log{r}\rangle}{\langle r\rangle^{2}},\quad|h^{\prime}(r)|\lesssim_{\lambda}\frac{\langle\log{r}\rangle}{r\langle r\rangle^{2}}.

(4.15)

We can then use the same argument as in Case 1 to prove that
\lambda\in(0,b(0))
can not be a discrete eigenvalue for
L_{k}
.

Case 3:
\lambda=b(0)
.
In this case, we note from (
4.2
), using (
1.10
) and (
1.12
), that for
r\in\mathbb{R}^{+}

\big(\partial_{r}^{2}\varphi+\frac{\partial_{r}\varphi}{r}-k^{2}r^{-2}\varphi\big)-\frac{\Omega^{\prime}(r)}{r(b(r)-b(0))}\varphi=0,

(4.16)

and

\frac{\Omega^{\prime}(r)}{r(b(r)-b(0))}>0.

(4.17)

By the maximum principle and the bounds (
4.4
), we must have
\varphi\equiv 0
, a contradiction with
\|g\|_{X_{k}}=1
. Therefore
\lambda=b(0)
cannot be a discrete eigenvalue.

Case 4:
\lambda=0
.
In this case, using (
4.2
) and (
4.4
) we obtain
h\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+})
and that for
r\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
,

|h(r)|\lesssim_{\lambda}\langle\log{r}\rangle,\quad|h^{\prime}(r)|\lesssim_{\lambda}\frac{\langle\log{r}\rangle}{r}.

(4.18)

We can then repeat the argument in Case 1, and conclude that
k=1
. The corresponding eigenfunction
g
for
\lambda=0
can be computed from
\varphi=-U(r)
, using (
1.2
) and (
4.1
) as

g=-\partial_{r}^{2}U-\frac{\partial_{r}U}{r}+k^{2}r^{-2}U=-\Omega^{\prime}(r).

Combining cases 1-4, we then complete the proof.
∎

5.
The limiting absorption principle

In this section we study the spectral density functions
\Pi^{+}_{k,\epsilon}(v,w)
and
\Pi^{-}_{k,\epsilon}(v,w),v,w\in\mathbb{R}
for
k\in\mathbb{Z}\backslash\{0\}
,
\epsilon>0
and sufficiently small. Recall from (
1.25
) that
\Pi^{\iota}_{k,\epsilon},\iota\in\{+,-\}
satisfy the equation that for
v,w\in\mathbb{R}
,

(k^{2}-\partial_{v}^{2})\Pi^{\iota}_{k,\epsilon}(v,w)+\frac{e^{2v}D(v)\Pi^{\iota}_{k,\epsilon}(v,w)}{B(v)-B(w)+i\iota\epsilon}=\frac{e^{2v}f_{0}^{k}(v)}{B(v)-B(w)+i\iota\epsilon}.

(5.1)

To study (
5.1
), we distinguish two cases:
w\geq-20
and
w\leq-10
.

For
w\geq-20
, we can reformulate (
5.1
) as

\Pi^{\iota}_{k,\epsilon}(v,w)+\frac{1}{2|k|}\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{-|k||v-\rho|}\frac{e^{2\rho}D(\rho)\Pi^{\iota}_{k,\epsilon}(\rho,w)}{B(\rho)-B(w)+i\iota\epsilon}\,d\rho=\frac{1}{2|k|}\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{-|k||v-\rho|}\frac{e^{2\rho}f_{0}^{k}(\rho)}{B(\rho)-B(w)+i\iota\epsilon}\,d\rho,

(5.2)

and we can bound
\Pi_{k,\epsilon}^{\iota}
by solving the integral equation using the spectral property proved in Proposition
4.1
.

For
w\leq-10
, the situation is trickier, and it is important to notice that the potential

\frac{e^{2v}D(v)}{B(v)-B(w)+i\iota\epsilon}\approx 8

for
v\in[w,0]
which is a large interval for
|w|\gg 1
. Therefore we need to incorporate part of the potential into the main term
k^{2}-\partial_{v}^{2}
, and, instead of using (
5.2
), we reformulate (
5.1
) as

\begin{split}&\Pi^{\iota}_{k,\epsilon}(v,w)+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{G}_{k}^{w}(v,\rho)\Big[\frac{e^{2\rho}D(\rho)}{B(\rho)-B(w)+i\iota\epsilon}-V_{w}(\rho)\Big]\Pi_{k,\epsilon}^{\iota}(\rho,w)\,d\rho\\
&=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{G}_{k}^{w}(v,\rho)\frac{e^{2\rho}f_{0}^{k}(\rho)}{B(\rho)-B(w)+i\iota\epsilon}\,d\rho.\end{split}

(5.3)

In the above we recall the definitions (
3.1
) and (
3.10
) for
V_{w}
and
\mathcal{G}_{k}^{w}
. (
5.3
) can be analyzed using the spectral property of
L_{k}
, see Proposition
4.1
, similar to the above, although the calculations are slightly more complicated.

5.1.
Limiting absorption principle for
w\geq-20
.

Define for
k\in\mathbb{Z}\backslash\{0\}
, and
k_{\ast}\in\mathbb{Z}
with
1\leq|k_{\ast}|\leq|k|
,

Y_{k,k_{\ast}}:=\bigg\{h\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}):\,\sup_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big[\big\|e^{|k_{\ast}||v|}h\big\|_{L^{2}(j,j+2)}+|k|^{-1}\big\|e^{|k_{\ast}||v|}\partial_{v}h\big\|_{L^{2}(j,j+2)}\Big]<\infty\bigg\},

(5.4)

with the natural norm

\|h\|_{Y_{k,k_{\ast}}}:=\sup_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big[\big\|e^{|k_{\ast}||v|}h\big\|_{L^{2}(j,j+2)}+|k|^{-1}\big\|e^{|k_{\ast}||v|}\partial_{v}h\big\|_{L^{2}(j,j+2)}\Big].

(5.5)

For
j\in\mathbb{Z}
, we also introduce for
h\in Y_{k,k_{\ast}}
, the norm

\|h\|_{Y_{k,k_{\ast}}^{j}}:=\big\|e^{|k_{\ast}||v|}h\big\|_{L^{2}(j,j+2)}+|k|^{-1}\big\|e^{|k_{\ast}||v|}\partial_{v}h\big\|_{L^{2}(j,j+2)}.

(5.6)

Define for
k\in\mathbb{Z}\backslash\{0\}
,
k_{\ast}\in\mathbb{Z}
with
1\leq|k_{\ast}|\leq|k|
and
\epsilon\in[-1/8,1/8]\backslash\{0\}
the operator
T_{k,\epsilon}^{w}:Y_{k,k_{\ast}}\to Y_{k,k_{\ast}}
as follows. For any
h\in Y_{k,k_{\ast}}
,

T_{k,\epsilon}^{w}h(v):=\frac{1}{2|k|}\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{-|k||v-\rho|}\frac{e^{2\rho}D(\rho)h(\rho)}{B(\rho)-B(w)+i\epsilon}\,d\rho.

(5.7)

Lemma 5.1
.

Assume that
w\geq-20
,
k\in\mathbb{Z}\backslash\{0\}
and
k_{\ast}\in\mathbb{Z}
with
1\leq|k_{\ast}|\leq|k|
. There exists
C\in(0,\infty)
which can be chosen independent of
w,k,k_{\ast}
, such that for
\epsilon\in[-e^{-2|w|},e^{-2|w|}]\backslash\{0\}
we have the following bounds for all
h\in Y_{k,k_{\ast}}
,

\left\|T_{k,\epsilon}^{w}h\right\|_{Y_{k,k_{\ast}}}\leq C|k|^{-1/5}\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{-|\ell|}\|h\|_{Y_{k,k_{\ast}}^{\ell}}\leq C|k|^{-1/5}\|h\|_{Y_{k,k_{\ast}}};

(5.8)

similarly,

\begin{split}&\sup_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big[\big\|e^{|k_{\ast}||v-w|}T_{k,\epsilon}^{w}h(v)\big\|_{L^{2}(j,j+2)}+|k|^{-1}\big\|e^{|k_{\ast}||v-w|}\partial_{v}T_{k,\epsilon}^{w}h(v)\big\|_{L^{2}(j,j+2)}\Big]\\
&\leq C|k|^{-1/5}\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{-|j|}\Big[\big\|e^{|k_{\ast}||v-w|}h\big\|_{L^{2}(j,j+2)}+|k|^{-1}\big\|e^{|k_{\ast}||v-w|}\partial_{v}h\big\|_{L^{2}(j,j+2)}\Big],\end{split}

(5.9)

and in addition, for
w\geq 0
,
v_{\ast}\in[0,w]
, we have

\begin{split}&\big\|e^{|k_{\ast}||v-w|}T_{k,\epsilon}^{w}h(v)\big\|_{L^{2}(v_{\ast},v_{\ast}+2)}+|k|^{-1}\big\|e^{|k_{\ast}||v-w|}\partial_{v}T_{k,\epsilon}^{w}h(v)\big\|_{L^{2}(v_{\ast},v_{\ast}+2)}\Big]\\
&\leq C|k|^{-1/5}e^{-|v_{\ast}|}\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{-|j|}\Big[\big\|e^{|k_{\ast}||v-w|}h\big\|_{L^{2}(j,j+2)}+|k|^{-1}\big\|e^{|k_{\ast}||v-w|}\partial_{v}h\big\|_{L^{2}(j,j+2)}\Big].\end{split}

(5.10)

Proof.

We focus on (
5.8
), as the proof of (
5.9
)-(
5.10
) is similar and we will indicate at the end of the proof the additional details that are needed.
We can assume that
k\geq 1
and
1\leq k_{\ast}\leq k
, without loss of generality. Choose a smooth cutoff function
\varphi\in C_{0}^{\infty}(-2,2)
with
\varphi\equiv 1
on
[-1,1]
. Set
\varphi_{k}(v):=\varphi(\sqrt{k}\,v),v\in\mathbb{R}
. We can write

\begin{split}T_{k,\epsilon}^{w}h(v)=\mathcal{J}_{1}h(v)+\mathcal{J}_{2}h(v)&:=\frac{1}{2k}\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{-k|v-\rho|}\varphi_{k}(\rho-w)\frac{e^{2\rho}D(\rho)}{B(\rho)-B(w)+i\epsilon}h(\rho)\,d\rho\\
&+\frac{1}{2k}\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{-k|v-\rho|}(1-\varphi_{k}(\rho-w))\frac{e^{2\rho}D(\rho)}{B(\rho)-B(w)+i\epsilon}h(\rho)\,d\rho\end{split}

(5.11)

In the above we have suppressed the dependence of
\mathcal{J}_{1}
and
\mathcal{J}_{2}
on
k,\epsilon
, for the simplicity of notations. We shall use this convention often, when there is no possibility of confusion. Setting
h^{\ast}(v)=e^{k_{\ast}|v|}h(v),v\in\mathbb{R}
, then for
j\in\mathbb{Z}
,

\|h^{\ast}\|_{L^{2}(j,j+2)}+k^{-1}\|\partial_{v}h^{\ast}\|_{L^{2}(j,j+2)}\lesssim\|h\|_{Y_{k,k_{\ast}}^{j}}.

(5.12)

Using

\begin{split}&|B(\rho)-B(w)|\gtrsim\frac{1}{\sqrt{k}}\frac{1}{1+e^{2\rho}},\,\,{\rm for}\,\,\rho<w-\frac{1}{\sqrt{k}},\\
&|B(\rho)-B(w)|\gtrsim e^{-2w}/\sqrt{k},\,\,{\rm for}\,\,\rho>w+\frac{1}{\sqrt{k}},\end{split}

(5.13)

we can bound

\begin{split}e^{|k_{\ast}||v|}\big|\mathcal{J}_{2}h(v)\big|&=\frac{1}{2k}\Big|\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{-k|v-\rho|}e^{k_{\ast}|v|}(1-\varphi_{k}(\rho-w))\frac{e^{2\rho}D(\rho)}{B(\rho)-B(w)+i\epsilon}h(\rho)\,d\rho\Big|\\
&\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{k}}\int_{-\infty}^{w-\frac{1}{\sqrt{k}}}\frac{e^{2\rho}}{1+e^{6\rho}}h^{\ast}(\rho)\,d\rho+\frac{1}{\sqrt{k}}\int_{w+\frac{1}{\sqrt{k}}}^{\infty}\frac{e^{2(\rho+w)}}{1+e^{8\rho}}h^{\ast}(\rho)\,d\rho.\end{split}

(5.14)

Similarly we can bound

k^{-1}e^{|k_{\ast}||v|}\big|\partial_{v}\big[\mathcal{J}_{2}h(v)\big]\big|\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{k}}\int_{-\infty}^{w-\frac{1}{\sqrt{k}}}\frac{e^{2\rho}}{1+e^{6\rho}}h^{\ast}(\rho)\,d\rho+\frac{1}{\sqrt{k}}\int_{w+\frac{1}{\sqrt{k}}}^{\infty}\frac{e^{2(\rho+w)}}{1+e^{8\rho}}h^{\ast}(\rho)\,d\rho.

(5.15)

It follows from (
5.14
) and (
5.15
) that

\big\|\mathcal{J}_{2}h\big\|_{Y_{k,k_{\ast}}}\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{k}}\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{-|j|}\|h\|_{Y_{k,k_{\ast}}^{j}}.

(5.16)

We can bound
\mathcal{J}_{1}h(v)
as follows,

\begin{split}&e^{k_{\ast}|v|}\big|\mathcal{J}_{1}h(v)\big|=\frac{1}{2k}\Big|\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{-k|v-\rho|}e^{k_{\ast}|v|}\varphi_{k}(\rho-w)\frac{e^{2\rho}D(\rho)}{B(\rho)-B(w)+i\epsilon}h(\rho)\,d\rho\Big|\\
&\lesssim\frac{1}{2k}\Big|\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{-k|v-\rho|}e^{k_{\ast}|v|}\varphi_{k}(\rho-w)\frac{e^{2\rho}D(\rho)}{B^{\prime}(\rho)}h(\rho)\partial_{\rho}\log{\frac{B(\rho)-B(w)+i\epsilon}{B^{\prime}(w)}}\,d\rho\Big|\\
&\lesssim\frac{1}{2k}\Big|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{\rho}\Big[e^{-k|v-\rho|}e^{k_{\ast}|v|}\varphi_{k}(\rho-w)\frac{e^{2\rho}D(\rho)}{B^{\prime}(\rho)}\Big]h(\rho)\log{\frac{B(\rho)-B(w)+i\epsilon}{B^{\prime}(w)}}\,d\rho\Big|\\
&\quad+\frac{1}{2k}\Big|\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{-k|v-\rho|}e^{k_{\ast}|v|}\varphi_{k}(\rho-w)\frac{e^{2\rho}D(\rho)}{B^{\prime}(\rho)}h^{\prime}(\rho)\log{\frac{B(\rho)-B(w)+i\epsilon}{B^{\prime}(w)}}\,d\rho\Big|\\
&\lesssim\int_{|\rho-w|<2/\sqrt{k}}\frac{e^{4\rho}}{1+e^{8\rho}}\Big[|h^{\ast}(\rho)|+e^{k_{\ast}|\rho|}|h^{\prime}(\rho)|/k\Big]\,\big\langle\log{|\rho-w|}\big\rangle\,d\rho.\end{split}

(5.17)

Similarly, using integration by parts, we have

\begin{split}&k^{-1}e^{k_{\ast}|v|}\big|\partial_{v}\big[\mathcal{J}_{1}h(v)\big]\big|=\frac{1}{2k^{2}}\Big|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{v}e^{-k|v-\rho|}e^{k_{\ast}|v|}\varphi_{k}(\rho-w)\frac{e^{2\rho}D(\rho)}{B(\rho)-B(w)+i\epsilon}h(\rho)\,d\rho\Big|\\
&\lesssim\frac{1}{2k^{2}}\Big|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{v}e^{-k|v-\rho|}e^{k_{\ast}|v|}\varphi_{k}(\rho-w)\frac{e^{2\rho}D(\rho)}{B^{\prime}(\rho)}h(\rho)\partial_{\rho}\log{\frac{B(\rho)-B(w)+i\epsilon}{B^{\prime}(w)}}\,d\rho\Big|\\
&\lesssim k^{-1}\big|D(v)e^{4v}\varphi_{k}(v-w)h^{\ast}(v)\big|\big\langle\log{|v-w|}\big\rangle+\int_{|\rho-w|<2/\sqrt{k}}\frac{e^{4\rho}}{1+e^{8\rho}}|h^{\ast}(\rho)|\big\langle\log|\rho-w|\big\rangle\,d\rho\\
&\qquad+k^{-1}\int_{|\rho-w|<2/\sqrt{k}}\frac{e^{4\rho}}{1+e^{8\rho}}e^{k_{\ast}|\rho|}|\partial_{\rho}h(\rho)|\big\langle\log|\rho-w|\big\rangle\,d\rho.\end{split}

(5.18)

It follows from (
5.17
) and (
5.18
), and Cauchy-Schwarz inequality that

\big\|\mathcal{J}_{1}h\big\|_{Y_{k,k_{\ast}}}\lesssim k^{-1/5}\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{-|j|}\|h\|_{Y_{k,k_{\ast}}^{j}}.

(5.19)

Combining (
5.16
) and (
5.19
), the proof of (
5.8
) is then complete.

The proof of (
5.9
) is similar and we only need the point-wise inequalities for
v,\rho\in\mathbb{R}
and
w\in[-20,\infty)
,

e^{k_{\ast}|v-w|}e^{-k|v-\rho|}\leq e^{k_{\ast}|\rho-w|}.

(5.20)

For (
5.10
) we only need the inequality for
w\geq 0,v\in[0,w]
and
\rho\in\mathbb{R}
,

e^{k_{\ast}|v-w|}e^{-k|v-\rho|}\lesssim e^{-|v|}e^{|\rho|}e^{k_{\ast}|\rho-w|}.

(5.21)

The proof is now complete.
∎

The following limiting absorption for
w\geq-20
plays an essential role in the study of the spectral density functions.

Lemma 5.2
.

Assume that
w\geq-20
,
k\in\mathbb{Z}\backslash\{0\}
and
k_{\ast}\in\mathbb{Z}
with
1\leq|k_{\ast}|\leq|k|
. There exist
\epsilon_{\ast}>0
sufficiently small, and
\kappa\in(0,\infty)
which can be chosen independent of
w,k,k_{\ast}
, such that the following statement holds. Suppose that
\epsilon\in\mathbb{R}
with
0<|\epsilon|<\epsilon_{\ast}e^{-2|w|}
, and
h\in Y_{k,k_{\ast}}
. If
|k|=1
, we assume in addition that
h
satisfies

\lim_{j\to\infty}\Big[\big\|e^{v}h\big\|_{L^{2}(j,j+1)}+\big\|e^{v}\partial_{v}h\big\|_{L^{2}(j,j+1)}\Big]=0.

(5.22)

Then we have

\left\|h+T_{k,\epsilon}^{w}h\right\|_{Y_{k,k_{\ast}}}\geq\kappa\|h\|_{Y_{k,k_{\ast}}},

(5.23)

and similarly

\begin{split}&\sup_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big[\big\|e^{|k_{\ast}||v-w|}\big(h+T_{k,\epsilon}^{w}h\big)(v)\big\|_{L^{2}(j,j+2)}+|k|^{-1}\big\|e^{|k_{\ast}||v-w|}\partial_{v}\big(h+T_{k,\epsilon}^{w}h\big)(v)\big\|_{L^{2}(j,j+2)}\Big]\\
&\geq\kappa\,\sup_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big[\big\|e^{|k_{\ast}||v-w|}h\big\|_{L^{2}(j,j+2)}+|k|^{-1}\big\|e^{|k_{\ast}||v-w|}\partial_{v}h\big\|_{L^{2}(j,j+2)}\Big].\end{split}

(5.24)

Proof.

We first give the proof of (
5.23
). We can assume that
k\geq 1
,
1\leq k_{\ast}\leq k
and
\|h\|_{Y_{k,k_{\ast}}}=1
.
By (
5.8
) we can assume that
1\leq k\leq L
for some
L\geq 1
, as the other case follows directly from (
5.8
). Suppose the bound does not hold, then there exist
k_{0}\in\mathbb{Z}\cap[1,L]
,
k_{\ast}\in\mathbb{Z}\cap[1,k_{0}]
,
h_{j}\in Y_{k_{0},k_{\ast}}
,
\|h_{j}\|_{Y_{k_{0},k_{\ast}}}=1
,
w_{j}\geq-20
, and
0\neq\epsilon_{j}\to 0
, for
j\geq 1
, such that

h_{j}(v)+\frac{1}{2k_{0}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{-k_{0}|v-\rho|}\frac{e^{2\rho}D(\rho)}{B(\rho)-B(w_{j})+i\epsilon_{j}}h_{j}(\rho)\,d\rho\to 0,

(5.25)

in
Y_{k_{0},k_{\ast}}
, as
j\to\infty
. We first note that for sufficiently large
K>1
,

\limsup_{j\to\infty}\|h_{j}\|_{H^{1}(-K,K)}>0,

(5.26)

which follows from (
5.8
) and (
5.25
). Using

\begin{split}\partial_{v}\big[T_{k_{0},\epsilon}^{w}h_{j}(v)\big]=&\frac{1}{2k_{0}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{v}e^{-k_{0}|v-\rho|}\big(1-\varphi_{k_{0}}(\rho-w)\big)\frac{e^{2\rho}D(\rho)}{B(\rho)-B(w_{j})+i\epsilon_{j}}h_{j}(\rho)\,d\rho\\
&+\frac{1}{2k_{0}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{v}e^{-k_{0}|v-\rho|}\varphi_{k_{0}}(\rho-w_{j})\frac{e^{2\rho}D(\rho)}{B(\rho)-B(w_{j})+i\epsilon_{j}}h_{j}(\rho)\,d\rho\\
:=&\mathcal{D}_{1}h_{j}(v)+\mathcal{D}_{2}h_{j}(v).\end{split}

(5.27)

For
\mathcal{D}_{1}h_{j}(v)
, we have

\begin{split}\partial_{v}\big[\mathcal{D}_{1}h_{j}(v)\big]=&-\big(1-\varphi_{k_{0}}(v-w_{j})\big)\frac{e^{2v}D(v)h_{j}(v)}{B(v)-B(w_{j})+i\epsilon}\\
&+\frac{k_{0}}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{-k_{0}|v-\rho|}(1-\varphi_{k_{0}}(\rho-w_{j}))\frac{e^{2\rho}D(\rho)}{B(\rho)-B(w_{j})+i\epsilon_{j}}h_{j}(\rho)\,d\rho,\end{split}

(5.28)

which can be bounded in
L^{2}(-K,K)
uniformly in
j\geq 1
, for any
K>1
. For the term
\mathcal{D}_{2}h_{j}(v)
, we have

\begin{split}\partial_{v}\big[&\mathcal{D}_{2}h_{j}(v)\big]=\frac{1}{2k_{0}}\partial_{v}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{v}e^{-k_{0}|v-\rho|}\varphi_{k_{0}}(\rho-w_{j})\frac{e^{2\rho}D(\rho)}{B^{\prime}(\rho)}h_{j}(\rho)\partial_{\rho}\log{\frac{B(\rho)-B(w_{j})+i\epsilon_{j}}{B^{\prime}(w_{j})}}\,d\rho\\
&=-\varphi_{k_{0}}(v-w_{j})\frac{e^{2v}D(v)}{B^{\prime}(v)}h_{j}(v)\partial_{v}\Big[\log{\frac{B(v)-B(w_{j})+i\epsilon_{j}}{B^{\prime}(w_{j})}}\Big]\\
&\quad-\frac{k_{0}}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{\rho}\Big[e^{-k_{0}|v-\rho|}\varphi_{k_{0}}(\rho-w_{j})\frac{e^{2\rho}D(\rho)}{B^{\prime}(\rho)}h_{j}(\rho)\Big]\log{\frac{B(\rho)-B(w_{j})+i\epsilon_{j}}{B^{\prime}(w_{j})}}\,d\rho,\end{split}

(5.29)

and as a consequence

\partial_{v}\Big[\mathcal{D}_{2}h_{j}(v)+\varphi_{k_{0}}(v-w_{j})\frac{e^{2v}D(v)}{B^{\prime}(v)}h_{j}(v)\log{\frac{B(v)-B(w_{j})+i\epsilon_{j}}{B^{\prime}(w_{j})}}\Big]

can be bounded
L^{2}(-K,K)
uniformly in
j\geq 1
, for any
K>1
. Therefore, we can pass to a subsequence and assume that
h_{j}\to h
in
H^{1}_{{\rm loc}}(\mathbb{R})
for some
h\in Y_{k_{0},k_{\ast}}(\mathbb{R})
with
h\not\equiv 0
.

To reach a contradiction, we consider two cases.

Case 1:
w_{j}\to w_{0}\in[-20,\infty)
.
Letting
j\to\infty
, we obtain from (
5.25
) that

h(v)+\frac{1}{2k_{0}}\lim_{j\to\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{-k_{0}|v-\rho|}\frac{e^{2\rho}D(\rho)}{B(\rho)-B(w_{0})+i\epsilon_{j}}h(\rho)\,d\rho=0,

(5.30)

and hence

(k_{0}^{2}-\partial_{v}^{2})h+\lim_{j\to\infty}\frac{e^{2v}D(v)h(v)}{B(v)-B(w_{0})+i\epsilon_{j}}=0.

(5.31)

Multiplying
\overline{h}
and integrating over
\mathbb{R}
, and taking the imaginary part, we get that
h(w_{0})=0
. Therefore

(k_{0}^{2}-\partial_{v}^{2})h+\frac{e^{2v}D(v)h(v)}{B(v)-B(w_{0})}=0.

(5.32)

Set

\phi(r)=h(v),\quad r=e^{v}\quad r_{0}=e^{w},\qquad{\rm for}\,\,v,w\in\mathbb{R}.

(5.33)

(
5.33
) can be reformulated as

\big(-\partial_{r}^{2}-\frac{\partial_{r}}{r}+k_{0}^{2}\,r^{-2}\big)\phi+\frac{d(r)}{b(r)-b(r_{0})}\phi=0,\quad{\rm for}\,\,r\in(0,\infty).

(5.34)

By
\phi(r_{0})=0
, standard elliptic regularity theory shows that
\phi\in C^{\infty}(0,\infty)
. Denote for
r\in(0,\infty)

g(r):=\big(-\partial_{r}^{2}-\frac{\partial_{r}}{r}+k_{0}^{2}\,r^{-2}\big)\phi(r)=-\frac{d(r)}{b(r)-b(r_{0})}\phi(r).

(5.35)

it follows from
h\in Y_{k_{0},k_{\ast}}
and
h(w_{0})=0
that

\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{r^{2}}{|\Omega^{\prime}(r)|}g^{2}(r)\,dr=\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{r}{|d(r)|}g^{2}(r)\,dr=\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{r|d(r)|}{|b(r)-b(r_{0})|^{2}}\phi^{2}(r)\,dr<\infty.

(5.36)

Therefore
g
is an eigenfunction of
L_{k_{0}}
with eigenvalue
\lambda=b(r_{0})
, a contradiction.

Case 2:
w_{j}\to\infty
.
Letting
j\to\infty
, we obtain that for
v\in\mathbb{R}
,

h(v)+\frac{1}{2k_{0}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{-k_{0}|v-\rho|}\frac{D(\rho)}{B(\rho)}e^{2\rho}h(\rho)\,d\rho=0,

(5.37)

or equivalently for
v\in\mathbb{R}
,

(k_{0}^{2}-\partial_{v}^{2})h(v)+\frac{e^{2v}D(v)h(v)}{B(v)}=0.

(5.38)

Setting for
r=e^{v},v\in\mathbb{R}
,

\phi(r):=h(v),\quad g(r):=\big(-\partial_{r}^{2}-\frac{\partial_{r}}{r}+k_{0}^{2}r^{-2}\big)\phi(r)=-\frac{d(r)}{b(r)}\phi(r),

(5.39)

then for
r\in\mathbb{R}^{+}

b(r)g(r)+d(r)\int_{0}^{\infty}G_{k_{0}}(r,\rho)g(\rho)\,d\rho=0.

(5.40)

It follows from
h\in Y_{k_{0},k_{\ast}}
that
g\in X_{k_{0}}
. Therefore
g
is an eigenfunction of
L_{k_{0}}
corresponding to the eigenvalue
\lambda=0
. By Proposition
4.1

k_{0}=1
and
g=-\Omega^{\prime}(r)
, which implies that
\phi(r)=U(r)\approx r^{-1}
as
r\to\infty
. So
h(v)\approx e^{-v}
as
v\to\infty
, a contradiction with (
5.22
). The proof of (
5.23
) is then complete.

We now turn to the proof of (
5.24
). The general idea is the same as in the proof of (
5.23
), using a contradiction argument. However, we need more refined estimates to extract a nontrivial limit since the weight used in this case requires us to renormalize the solution by multiplying a large constant. We can assume that
k\geq 1
,
1\leq k_{\ast}\leq k
and

\sup_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big[\big\|e^{|k_{\ast}||v-w|}h\big\|_{L^{2}(l,l+2)}+|k|^{-1}\big\|e^{|k_{\ast}||v-w|}\partial_{v}h\big\|_{L^{2}(l,l+2)}\Big]=1.

By (
5.9
) we can assume that
1\leq k\leq L
for some
L\geq 1
, as the other case follows directly from (
5.9
). Suppose (
5.24
) does not hold, then there exist
k_{0}\in\mathbb{Z}\cap[1,L]
,
k_{\ast}\in\mathbb{Z}\cap[1,k_{0}]
, and for
j\geq 1
,
0\neq\epsilon_{j}\to 0
,
w_{j}\geq-20
, and
h_{j}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R})
satisfying

\sup_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big[\big\|e^{|k_{\ast}||v-w_{j}|}h_{j}\big\|_{L^{2}(l,l+2)}+|k_{0}|^{-1}\big\|e^{|k_{\ast}||v-w_{j}|}\partial_{v}h_{j}\big\|_{L^{2}(l,l+2)}\Big]=1,

such that

\begin{split}&\sup_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big[\big\|e^{|k_{\ast}||v-w_{j}|}\big(h_{j}+T_{k_{0},\epsilon_{j}}^{w_{j}}h_{j}\big)(v)\big\|_{L^{2}(l,l+2)}+|k_{0}|^{-1}\big\|e^{|k_{\ast}||v-w_{j}|}\partial_{v}\big(h_{j}+T_{k_{0},\epsilon_{j}}^{w_{j}}h_{j}\big)(v)\big\|_{L^{2}(l,l+2)}\Big]\\
&\to 0+,\qquad{\rm as}\,\,j\to\infty.\end{split}

(5.41)

We first note, as a consequence of (
5.9
), that for some sufficiently large
K>1
and all large
j\gg 1
,

\limsup_{j\to\infty}\big\|e^{k_{\ast}|v-w_{j}|}h_{j}(v)\big\|_{H^{1}(-K,K)}\gtrsim 1.

(5.42)

It suffices to consider the case
w_{j}\to\infty
, as the other case follows from the same argument as in the proof of (
5.23
), case 1.
We write for
j\geq 1

h_{j}=-T_{k_{0},\epsilon_{j}}^{w_{j}}h_{j}+r_{j},

(5.43)

where the functions
r_{j}(v),v\in\mathbb{R}
satisfy as
j\to\infty

\sup_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big[\big\|e^{|k_{\ast}||v-w_{j}|}r_{j}(v)\big\|_{L^{2}(l,l+2)}+|k_{0}|^{-1}\big\|e^{|k_{\ast}||v-w_{j}|}\partial_{v}r_{j}(v)\big\|_{L^{2}(l,l+2)}\Big]\to 0+.

(5.44)

By (
5.10
), we have for
v_{\ast}\in[0,w_{j}]
that

\Big[\big\|e^{|k_{\ast}||v-w_{j}|}T_{k_{0},\epsilon_{j}}^{w_{j}}h_{j}(v)\big\|_{L^{2}(v_{\ast},v_{\ast}+2)}+|k_{0}|^{-1}\big\|e^{|k_{\ast}||v-w_{j}|}\partial_{v}T_{k_{0},\epsilon_{j}}^{w_{j}}h_{j}(v)\big\|_{L^{2}(v_{\ast},v_{\ast}+2)}\Big]\lesssim e^{-|v_{\ast}|}.

(5.45)

Therefore, defining for
v\in\mathbb{R}
,

g_{j}(v):=e^{|k_{\ast}||w_{j}|}h_{j}(v),

(5.46)

we can conclude that
g_{j}
are bounded in
H^{1}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R})
uniformly in
j
. Using calculations similar to (
5.27
)-(
5.29
) to obtain compactness in
H^{1}_{\rm loc}
, by passing to a subsequence, we can assume
g_{j}\to g
in
H^{1}_{\rm loc}
as
j\to\infty
, for some

g\in H^{1}_{\rm loc}
with
g\not\equiv 0
and for
v_{\ast}\in\mathbb{R}

\Big[\big\|g\big\|_{L^{2}(v_{\ast},v_{\ast}+2)}+|k_{0}|^{-1}\big\|\partial_{v}g\big\|_{L^{2}(v_{\ast},v_{\ast}+2)}\Big]\lesssim e^{-|k_{\ast}||v_{\ast}|}\mathbf{1}_{v_{\ast}<0}+e^{(|k_{\ast}|-1)|v_{\ast}|}\mathbf{1}_{v_{\ast}>0}.

(5.47)

It follows from (
5.43
)-(
5.44
) that for
v\in\mathbb{R}
,

(k_{0}^{2}-\partial_{v}^{2})g(v)+\frac{e^{2v}D(v)}{B(v)}g(v)=0.

(5.48)

By (
5.47
) and the comparison principle, noting that
\frac{e^{2v}D(v)}{B(v)}\to 0
as
v\to\infty
, we conclude that if we choose
R>1
sufficiently large, then for all
\sigma>0
and
v\geq R
,

|g(v)|\leq\sigma\,e^{(|k_{0}|-1/2)v}+|g(R)|e^{-(|k_{0}|-1/2)(v-R)},

(5.49)

which together with the bounds (
5.47
) (for
v_{\ast}<0
) and the equation (
5.48
) imply that for
v\in\mathbb{R}
,

\big|g(v)\big|+\big|\partial_{v}g(v)\big|\lesssim_{k_{0}}e^{-|k_{0}||v|},

(5.50)

and we can obtain a contradiction as in the proof (
5.23
), Case 2. The proof of (
5.24
) is now complete.

∎

Remark 5.3
.

We briefly explain the motivation behind the assumption (
5.22
) for
|k|=1
.
Setting for
r,r_{0}\in(0,\infty)
,

g_{k,\epsilon}^{\iota}(r,r_{0}):=\big(-\partial_{r}^{2}-\frac{\partial_{r}}{r}+k^{2}r^{-2}\big)\psi_{k,\epsilon}^{\iota}(r,r_{0}),

(5.51)

then from (
1.21
) we have for
r,r_{0}\in(0,\infty)
,

\big[L_{k}g_{k,\epsilon}^{\iota}(\cdot,r_{0})\big](r)+(i\iota\epsilon-b(r_{0}))g_{k,\epsilon}^{\iota}(r,r_{0})=\omega_{0}^{k}(r).

(5.52)

By the orthogonality of
\omega_{0}^{k}
and
\Omega^{\prime}(r)
with respect to the
L^{2}\big(\mathbb{R}^{+},\frac{r^{2}}{|\Omega^{\prime}(r)|}dr\big)
metric, see (
1.29
), and the fact that
L_{k}
is self adjoint in this metric, we see that
g_{k,\epsilon}^{\iota}(r,r_{0})
is orthogonal to
\Omega^{\prime}(r)
in
X_{k}
, that is

\int_{0}^{\infty}g_{k,\epsilon}^{\iota}(r,r_{0})r^{2}\,dr=0,

(5.53)

which implies that

\psi_{k,\epsilon}^{\iota}(r,r_{0})=o\big(1/r\big),\quad{\rm as}\,\,r\to\infty.

(5.54)

Recall the relation
\Pi_{k,\epsilon}^{\iota}(v,w)=\psi_{k,\epsilon}^{\iota}(r,r_{0})
, we see that

\lim_{v\to\infty}\big[e^{v}\Pi_{k,\epsilon}^{\iota}(v,w)\big]=0.

(5.55)

Hence we can apply Lemma
5.2
to obtain bounds on
\Pi_{k,\epsilon}^{\iota}(v,w)
for
|k|=1
.

5.2.
The limiting absorption principle for
w\leq-10
.

This case is more involved since the lower order term is “long range” and a non-local contribution has to be extracted in order to consider it perturbative. Define for
w\leq-10
,
k\in\mathbb{Z}\backslash\{0\}
,
k_{\ast}\in\mathbb{Z}
with
1\leq|k_{\ast}|\leq|k|
, and
\epsilon\in[-e^{2w},e^{2w}]\backslash\{0\}
the space

Y_{k,k_{\ast},w}:=\Big\{h\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}):\,\sup_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big[\big\|\varrho_{k_{\ast},w}(v)h(v)\big\|_{L^{2}(j,j+2)}+|k|^{-1}\big\|\varrho_{k_{\ast},w}(v)\partial_{v}h(v)\big\|_{L^{2}(j,j+2)}\Big]<\infty\Big\},

(5.56)

and the operator
S_{k,\epsilon}^{w}:Y_{k,k_{\ast},w}\to Y_{k,k_{\ast},w}
, for any
h\in Y_{k,k_{\ast},w}
,

S_{k,\epsilon}^{w}h(v):=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{G}_{k}^{w}(v,\rho)\Big[\frac{e^{2\rho}D(\rho)}{B(\rho)-B(w)+i\epsilon}-V_{w}(\rho)\Big]h(\rho)\,d\rho.

(5.57)

In the above we recall that

\varrho_{k_{\ast},w}(v):=\zeta_{k_{\ast},w}(v,0)=\frac{1}{\varpi_{k_{\ast},w}(v,0)}.

(5.58)

For
w\leq-10
,
k\in\mathbb{Z}\backslash\{0\}
and
k_{\ast}\in\mathbb{Z}
with
1\leq|k_{\ast}|\leq|k|
, we also define for
j\in\mathbb{Z}
and
h\in Y_{k,k_{\ast},w}
the norm

\|h\|_{Y_{k,k_{\ast},w}^{j}}:=\big\|\varrho_{k_{\ast},w}(v)h(v)\big\|_{L^{2}(j,j+2)}+|k|^{-1}\big\|\varrho_{k_{\ast},w}(v)\partial_{v}h(v)\big\|_{L^{2}(j,j+2)}.

(5.59)

For applications below we first establish the following bounds.

Lemma 5.4
.

Suppose
w\leq-10
,
k\in\mathbb{Z}\backslash\{0\}
,
k_{\ast}\in\mathbb{Z}
with
1\leq|k_{\ast}|\leq|k|
, and
\epsilon\in[-e^{2w},e^{2w}]\backslash\{0\}
. For
\rho,v\in\mathbb{R}
with
|\rho-w|>1/\sqrt{|k|}
, we have the point-wise inequalities

\varrho_{k_{\ast},w}(v)\mathcal{G}_{k}^{w}(v,\rho)\Big|\frac{e^{2\rho}D(\rho)}{B(\rho)-B(w)+i\epsilon}-V_{w}(\rho)\Big|\lesssim|k|^{-1/2}e^{-2|\rho-w|}\varrho_{k_{\ast},w}(\rho),

(5.60)

\varrho_{k_{\ast},w}(v)\big|\partial_{v}\mathcal{G}_{k}^{w}(v,\rho)\big|\Big|\frac{e^{2\rho}D(\rho)}{B(\rho)-B(w)+i\epsilon}-V_{w}(\rho)\Big|\lesssim|k|^{1/2}e^{-2|\rho-w|}\varrho_{k_{\ast},w}(\rho),

(5.61)

and for
\rho,v\in\mathbb{R}
we have

\varrho_{k_{\ast},w}(v)\mathcal{G}_{k}^{w}(v,\rho)\lesssim\varrho_{k_{\ast},w}(\rho)/|k|,\qquad\varrho_{k_{\ast},w}(v)\big|\partial_{v}\mathcal{G}_{k}^{w}(v,\rho)\big|\lesssim\varrho_{k_{\ast},w}(\rho).

(5.62)

We also record the useful estimate for
\rho>w+5
,

\Big|\frac{e^{2\rho}D(\rho)}{B(\rho)-B(w)+i\epsilon}-V_{w}(\rho)\Big|\lesssim\frac{e^{-2\rho}\epsilon}{(1+e^{\rho})^{4}}.

(5.63)

Proof.

Recall the bounds (
1.35
)-(
1.36
). Therefore, we have for
\rho<w-1/\sqrt{k}
,

\Big|\frac{e^{2\rho}D(\rho)}{B(\rho)-B(w)+i\epsilon}-V_{w}(\rho)\Big|\lesssim\sqrt{k}\,e^{-2|w-\rho|};

(5.64)

for
\rho\in[w+1/\sqrt{k},w+5]
,

\Big|\frac{e^{2\rho}D(\rho)}{B(\rho)-B(w)+i\epsilon}-V_{w}(\rho)\Big|\lesssim\sqrt{k};

(5.65)

for
\rho>w+5
,

\Big|\frac{e^{2\rho}D(\rho)}{B(\rho)-B(w)+i\epsilon}-V_{w}(\rho)\Big|\lesssim\frac{e^{-2\rho}\epsilon}{(1+e^{\rho})^{4}}\lesssim\frac{e^{-2|\rho-w|}}{(1+e^{\rho})^{4}}.

(5.66)

(
5.60
) then follows from (
5.64
)-(
5.66
) and (
5.62
), which follows from simple calculations, the bounds (
3.11
)-(
3.12
) on the Green’s function
\mathcal{G}_{k}^{w}(v,\rho)
and the definition (
5.58
) of
\varrho_{k_{\ast},w}(v)
for
v,\rho\in\mathbb{R}
.

∎

Now we can prove bounds on
S_{k,\epsilon}^{w}
.

Lemma 5.5
.

Assume that
w\leq-10
,
k\in\mathbb{Z}\backslash\{0\}
,
k_{\ast}\in\mathbb{Z}
with
1\leq|k_{\ast}|\leq|k|
, and
\epsilon\in[-e^{2w},e^{2w}]\backslash\{0\}
. Then there exist
C\in(0,\infty)
which can be chosen independent of
w,k,k_{\ast}
and
\epsilon
, such that the following statement holds. For
h\in Y_{k,w}
, we have the bounds

\big\|S_{k,\epsilon}^{w}h\big\|_{Y_{k,k_{\ast},w}}\leq C|k|^{-1/5}\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{-|j-w|}\|h\|_{Y^{j}_{k,k_{\ast},w}},

(5.67)

and similarly

\begin{split}&\sup_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big[\big\|\zeta_{k_{\ast},w}(v,w)S_{k,\epsilon}^{w}h(v)\big\|_{L^{2}(j,j+2)}+|k|^{-1}\big\|\zeta_{k_{\ast},w}(v,w)\partial_{v}S_{k,\epsilon}^{w}h(v)\big\|_{L^{2}(j,j+2)}\Big]\\
&\leq C|k|^{-1/5}\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{-|j-w|}\Big[\big\|\zeta_{k_{\ast},w}(v,w)h(v)\big\|_{L^{2}(j,j+2)}+|k|^{-1}\big\|\zeta_{k_{\ast},w}(v,w)\partial_{v}h(v)\big\|_{L^{2}(j,j+2)}\Big].\end{split}

(5.68)

Proof.

We focus on the proof of (
5.67
), as the proof of (
5.68
) is similar and we will indicate the required changes at the end of the proof. We assume without loss of generality that
k\geq 1
and
1\leq k_{\ast}\leq k
. Choose smooth cutoff function
\varphi\in C_{0}^{\infty}(-2,2)
with
\varphi\equiv 1
on
[-1,1]
, and set
\varphi_{k}(v)=\varphi(\sqrt{k}\,v)
for
v\in\mathbb{R}
. Let

\begin{split}\mathcal{J}_{1}h(v)&=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{G}_{k}^{w}(v,\rho)\Big[\frac{e^{2\rho}D(\rho)}{B(\rho)-B(w)+i\epsilon}-V_{w}(\rho)\Big](1-\varphi_{k}(\rho-w))h(\rho)\,d\rho,\\
\mathcal{J}_{2}h(v)&=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{G}_{k}^{w}(v,\rho)\Big[\frac{e^{2\rho}D(\rho)}{B(\rho)-B(w)+i\epsilon}-V_{w}(\rho)\Big]\varphi_{k}(\rho-w)h(\rho)\,d\rho.\end{split}

(5.69)

In the above, as usual, we suppressed the dependence of
\mathcal{J}_{1},\mathcal{J}_{2}
on
k,w,\epsilon
, for the simplicity of notations.

Since
S_{k,\epsilon}^{w}h=\mathcal{J}_{1}h+\mathcal{J}_{2}h
, it suffices to bound
\mathcal{J}_{1}h
and
\mathcal{J}_{2}h
.
Using (
5.60
)-(
5.61
), we can bound

\varrho_{k_{\ast},w}(v)\big|\mathcal{J}_{1}h(v)\big|+k^{-1}\varrho_{k_{\ast},w}(v)\big|\partial_{v}\mathcal{J}_{1}h(v)\big|\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{k}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{-|\rho-w|}\varrho_{k_{\ast},w}(\rho)|h(\rho)|\,d\rho,

(5.70)

which implies that

\big\|\mathcal{J}_{1}h\big\|_{Y_{k,k_{\ast},w}}\lesssim Ck^{-1/2}\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{-|j-w|}\|h\|_{Y^{j}_{k,k_{\ast},w}}.

(5.71)

It suffices to bound
\mathcal{J}_{2}h(v)
. We write

\begin{split}\mathcal{J}_{2}h(v)=\mathcal{J}_{21}h(v)+\mathcal{J}_{22}h(v):=&\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{G}_{k}^{w}(v,\rho)\big[-V_{w}(\rho)\big]\varphi_{k}(\rho-w)h(\rho)\,d\rho\\
&+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{G}_{k}^{w}(v,\rho)\Big[\frac{e^{2\rho}D(\rho)}{B(\rho)-B(w)+i\epsilon}\Big]\varphi_{k}(\rho-w)h(\rho)\,d\rho.\end{split}

(5.72)

Using the bound (
5.62
), we can bound

\begin{split}\varrho_{k_{\ast},w}(v)\big|\mathcal{J}_{21}h(v)\big|+k^{-1}\varrho_{k_{\ast},w}(v)\big|\mathcal{J}_{21}h(v)\big|\lesssim k^{-1}\int_{|\rho-w|\leq 2/\sqrt{k}}\varrho_{k_{\ast},w}(\rho)|h(\rho)|\,d\rho,\end{split}

(5.73)

which implies that

\big\|\mathcal{J}_{21}h\big\|_{Y_{k,k_{\ast},w}}\lesssim C|k|^{-1}\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{-|j-w|}\|h\|_{Y^{j}_{k,k_{\ast},w}}.

(5.74)

It remains to bound the term
\mathcal{J}_{22}h
. Using (
5.60
)-(
5.61
) and (
1.35
), we obtain

\begin{split}&\varrho_{k_{\ast},w}(v)\Big|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{G}_{k}^{w}(v,\rho)\frac{e^{2\rho}D(\rho)}{B(\rho)-B(w)+i\epsilon}\varphi_{k}(\rho-w)h(\rho)\,d\rho\Big|\\
&\lesssim\varrho_{k_{\ast},w}(v)\Big|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{\rho}\Big[\mathcal{G}_{k}^{w}(v,\rho)\frac{e^{2\rho}D(\rho)}{\partial_{\rho}B(\rho)}\varphi_{k}(\rho-w)\Big]h(\rho)\log\frac{B(\rho)-B(w)+i\epsilon}{\partial_{w}B(w)}\,d\rho\Big|\\
&\quad+\varrho_{k_{\ast},w}(v)\Big|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{G}_{k}^{w}(v,\rho)\frac{e^{2\rho}D(\rho)}{\partial_{\rho}B(\rho)}\varphi_{k}(\rho-w)\partial_{\rho}h(\rho)\log\frac{B(\rho)-B(w)+i\epsilon}{\partial_{w}B(w)}\,d\rho\Big|\\
&\lesssim\int_{|\rho-w|<2/\sqrt{k}}\varrho_{k_{\ast},w}(\rho)|h(\rho)|\langle\log{|\rho-w|}\rangle\,d\rho+\frac{1}{k}\int_{|\rho-w|<2/\sqrt{k}}\varrho_{k_{\ast},w}(\rho)|\partial_{\rho}h(\rho)|\langle\log{|\rho-w|}\rangle\,d\rho.\end{split}

(5.75)

In addition, in view of (
3.13
),

\begin{split}&k^{-1}\varrho_{k_{\ast},w}(v)\Big|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{v}\mathcal{G}_{k}^{w}(v,\rho)\frac{e^{2\rho}D(\rho)}{B(\rho)-B(w)+i\epsilon}\varphi_{k}(\rho-w)h(\rho)\,d\rho\Big|\\
&\lesssim k^{-1}\varrho_{k_{\ast},w}(v)\Big|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{\rho}\Big[\partial_{v}\mathcal{G}_{k}^{w}(v,\rho)\frac{e^{2\rho}D(\rho)}{\partial_{\rho}B(\rho)}\varphi_{k}(\rho-w)\Big]h(\rho)\log\frac{B(\rho)-B(w)+i\epsilon}{\partial_{w}B(w)}\,d\rho\Big|\\
&\quad+k^{-1}\varrho_{k_{\ast},w}(v)\Big|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{v}\mathcal{G}_{k}^{w}(v,\rho)\frac{e^{2\rho}D(\rho)}{\partial_{\rho}B(\rho)}\varphi_{k}(\rho-w)\partial_{\rho}h(\rho)\log\frac{B(\rho)-B(w)+i\epsilon}{\partial_{w}B(w)}\,d\rho\Big|\\
&\lesssim k^{-1}\varrho_{k_{\ast},w}(v)\varphi_{k}(v-w)|h(v)|\langle\log{|v-w|}\rangle+\int_{|\rho-w|<2/\sqrt{k}}\varrho_{k_{\ast},w}(\rho)|h(\rho)|\langle\log{|\rho-w|}\rangle\,d\rho\\
&\quad+k^{-1}\int_{|\rho-w|<2/\sqrt{k}}\varrho_{k_{\ast},w}(\rho)|\partial_{\rho}h(\rho)|\langle\log{|\rho-w|}\rangle\,d\rho.\\
\end{split}

(5.76)

It follows from (
5.75
) and (
5.76
) that

\big\|\mathcal{J}_{22}h\big\|_{Y_{k,k_{\ast},w}}\lesssim C|k|^{-1/5}\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{-|j-w|}\|h\|_{Y^{j}_{k,k_{\ast},w}}.

(5.77)

Combining (
5.71
), (
5.74
) and (
5.77
), the proof of (
5.67
) is then complete.

The proof of (
5.68
) is similar and we only need to use

\zeta_{k,w}(v,w)\mathcal{G}_{k}^{w}(v,\rho)\lesssim\zeta_{k,w}(\rho,w)/|k|,\qquad\zeta_{k,w}(v,w)\big|\partial_{v}\mathcal{G}_{k}^{w}(v,\rho)\big|\lesssim\zeta_{k,w}(\rho,w).

(5.78)

The lemma is now proved.

∎

We have the following limiting absorption principle for
w\leq-10
.

Lemma 5.6
.

Assume that
w\leq-10
,
k\in\mathbb{Z}\backslash\{0\}
and
k_{\ast}\in\mathbb{Z}
with
1\leq|k_{\ast}|\leq|k|
. Then there exist
\kappa\in(0,\infty)
and a sufficiently small
\beta_{\ast}\in(0,\infty)
, which can be chosen independent of
w,k
and
k_{\ast}
, such that the following statement holds. For
\epsilon\in[-\beta_{\ast}e^{2w},\beta_{\ast}e^{2w}]\backslash\{0\}
and
h\in Y_{k,k_{\ast},w}
, we have

\big\|h+S_{k,\epsilon}^{w}h\big\|_{Y_{k,k_{\ast},w}}\geq\kappa\|h\|_{Y_{k,k_{\ast},w}},

(5.79)

and similarly

\begin{split}&\sup_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big[\big\|\zeta_{k_{\ast},w}(v,w)(h+S_{k,\epsilon}^{w}h)(v)\big\|_{L^{2}(j,j+2)}+|k|^{-1}\big\|\zeta_{k_{\ast},w}(v,w)\partial_{v}(h+S_{k,\epsilon}^{w}h)(v)\big\|_{L^{2}(j,j+2)}\Big]\\
&\geq\kappa\sup_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big[\big\|\zeta_{k_{\ast},w}(v,w)h(v)\big\|_{L^{2}(j,j+2)}+|k|^{-1}\big\|\zeta_{k_{\ast},w}(v,w)\partial_{v}h(v)\big\|_{L^{2}(j,j+2)}\Big].\end{split}

(5.80)

Proof.

We focus on (
5.79
), as the proof of (
5.80
) is easier. We can assume
k\geq 1
and
1\leq k_{\ast}\leq k
. Suppose (
5.79
) does not hold. Then by Lemma
5.6
, there exist
k_{0}\geq 1
and
k_{\ast}\in\mathbb{Z}
with
1\leq k_{\ast}\leq k_{0}
,
w_{j}\leq-10
,
\beta_{j}\neq 0,\beta_{j}\to 0
as
j\to\infty
,
h_{j}\in Y_{k_{0},k_{\ast},w_{j}}
,
\|h_{j}\|_{Y_{k_{0},k_{\ast},w_{j}}}=1
, such that with
\epsilon_{j}:=\beta_{j}e^{2w_{j}}
, as
j\to\infty
we have

h_{j}+S_{k_{0},\epsilon_{j}}^{w_{j}}h_{j}\to 0,\qquad{\rm in}\,\,Y_{k_{0},k_{\ast},w_{j}}.

(5.81)

By Lemma
5.6
, we can find some
\delta>0,K>1
, such that

\big\|\varrho_{k_{\ast},w}(v)h_{j}(v)\big\|_{L^{2}(w_{j}-K,w_{j}+K)}+\big\|\varrho_{k_{\ast},w}(v)\partial_{v}h_{j}(v)\big\|_{L^{2}(w_{j}-K,w_{j}+K)}>\delta,

(5.82)

for sufficiently large
j\geq 1
. If
w_{j}
remains bounded for a subsequence, then we can obtain a contradiction as in the proof of Lemma
5.2
, case 1. It suffices to assume that
w_{j}\to-\infty
. To pass to a nontrivial limit, we need to obtain more refined bounds on
h_{j}
,
j\geq 0
.

Write for
j\geq 1
,

h_{j}=-S_{k_{0},\epsilon_{j}}^{w_{j}}h_{j}+r_{j},\qquad{\rm where}\,\,r_{j}\in Y_{k_{0},k_{\ast},w_{j}}\,\,{\rm and}\,\,\lim_{j\to+\infty}\|r_{j}\|_{Y_{k_{0},k_{\ast},w_{j}}}=0.

(5.83)

Fix
\Phi_{\leq 6}\in C_{0}^{\infty}(-\infty,8)
with
\Phi_{\leq 6}\equiv 1
on
(-\infty,6]
. We define

\begin{split}\mathcal{J}_{3}h_{j}(v)&:=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{G}_{k}^{w_{j}}(v,\rho)\Big[\frac{e^{2\rho}D(\rho)}{B(\rho)-B(w_{j})+i\epsilon_{j}}-V_{w_{j}}(\rho)\Big]\big(1-\Phi_{\leq 6}(\rho-w_{j})\big)h_{j}(\rho)\,d\rho,\\
\mathcal{J}_{4}h_{j}(v)&:=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{G}_{k}^{w_{j}}(v,\rho)\Big[\frac{e^{2\rho}D(\rho)}{B(\rho)-B(w_{j})+i\epsilon_{j}}-V_{w_{j}}(\rho)\Big]\Phi_{\leq 6}(\rho-w_{j})h_{j}(\rho)\,d\rho,\end{split}

(5.84)

and it follows that

S_{k_{0},\epsilon_{j}}^{w_{j}}h_{j}=\mathcal{J}_{3}h_{j}(v)+\mathcal{J}_{4}h_{j}(v).

(5.85)

Using the bounds (
5.63
), we obtain that

\big\|\mathcal{J}_{3}h_{j}(v)\big\|_{Y_{k_{0},k_{\ast},w_{j}}}\lesssim_{k_{0}}|\epsilon_{j}|e^{-2w_{j}}\big\|h_{j}\big\|_{Y_{k_{0},k_{\ast},w_{j}}}=|\beta_{j}|\to 0+,\quad{\rm as}\,\,j\to\infty.

(5.86)

To obtain more accurate bounds on
\mathcal{J}_{4}h_{j}
than those from Lemma
5.67
, that are needed for extracting nontrivial limiting profile from the sequence
h_{j}
, we fix
\varphi\in C_{0}^{\infty}(-20,20)
with
\varphi\equiv 1
on
[-10,10]
and note that

\begin{split}&\big|\mathcal{J}_{4}h_{j}(v)\big|+\big|\partial_{v}\mathcal{J}_{4}h_{j}(v)\big|\lesssim_{k_{0}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\big(\big|\mathcal{G}_{k_{0}}^{w_{j}}(v,\rho)\big|+\big|\partial_{v}\mathcal{G}_{k_{0}}^{w_{j}}(v,\rho)\big|\big)\big|V_{w_{j}}(\rho)\Phi_{\leq 6}(\rho-w_{j})h_{j}(\rho)\big|\,d\rho\\
&+\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{\rho}\Big[\frac{e^{2\rho}D(\rho)}{\partial_{\rho}B(\rho)}\mathcal{G}_{k_{0}}^{w_{j}}(v,\rho)\Phi_{\leq 6}(\rho-w_{j})\varphi(\rho-w_{j})h_{j}(\rho)\Big]\log{\frac{B(\rho)-B(w_{j})+i\epsilon_{j}}{\partial_{w}B(w_{j})}}\,d\rho\right|\\
&+\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{\rho}\Big[\frac{e^{2\rho}D(\rho)}{\partial_{\rho}B(\rho)}\partial_{v}\mathcal{G}_{k_{0}}^{w_{j}}(v,\rho)\Phi_{\leq 6}(\rho-w_{j})\varphi(\rho-w_{j})h_{j}(\rho)\Big]\log{\frac{B(\rho)-B(w_{j})+i\epsilon_{j}}{\partial_{w}B(w_{j})}}\,d\rho\right|\\
&+\int_{-\infty}^{w_{j}-1}\big(\big|\mathcal{G}_{k_{0}}^{w_{j}}(v,\rho)\big|+\big|\partial_{v}\mathcal{G}_{k_{0}}^{w_{j}}(v,\rho)\big|\big)|1-\varphi(\rho-w_{j})|e^{-2|\rho-w_{j}|}\big|h_{j}(\rho)\big|\,d\rho.\end{split}

(5.87)

We obtain from (
5.87
) that for
v_{\ast}>w_{j}+9
,

\begin{split}&\big\|\big|\mathcal{J}_{4}h_{j}(v)\big|+\big|\partial_{v}\mathcal{J}_{4}h_{j}(v)\big|\big\|_{L^{2}(v_{\ast},v_{\ast}+2)}\lesssim\frac{\varpi_{k_{\ast},w_{j}}(v_{\ast},w_{j})}{\varrho_{k_{\ast},w_{j}}(w_{j})}.\end{split}

(5.88)

Setting

g_{j}(v)=e^{\mu_{k_{\ast}}|w_{j}|}h_{j}(w_{j}+v),\qquad v\in\mathbb{R},

(5.89)

then combining (
5.82
)-(
5.83
), (
5.86
) and (
5.88
) we obtain that

\|g_{j}\|_{H^{1}(-K,K)}>\delta,\qquad{\rm and}\,\,g_{j}\to g\,\,{\rm in}\,\,H^{1}_{{\rm loc}}(-L,L),\,\,{\rm for\,\,all\,\,}L>1.

(5.90)

We note that the compactness in
H^{1}_{{\rm loc}}(\mathbb{R})
of
g_{j}
follows from similar argument as in the case of
w>-20
.
In addition,
g
satisfies

\sup_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big[\big\|e^{|v|}g\big\|_{L^{2}(j,j+2)}+\big\|e^{|v|}\partial_{v}g\big\|_{L^{2}(j,j+2)}\Big]<\infty.

(5.91)

Since in the sense of distributions for
v\in\mathbb{R}
,

(k_{0}^{2}-\partial_{v}^{2}+V_{w_{j}}(w_{j}+v))g_{j}(v)+\Big[\frac{e^{2v+2w_{j}}D(w_{j}+v)}{B(w_{j}+v)-B(w_{j})+i\epsilon_{j}}-V_{w_{j}}(w_{j}+v)\Big]g_{j}(v)=0,

(5.92)

sending
j\to\infty
and using (
1.35
)-(
1.36
), we obtain that for
v\in\mathbb{R}
,

(k^{2}-\partial_{v}^{2})g(v)+\lim_{j\to\infty}\frac{8e^{2v}}{e^{2v}-1+8i\beta_{j}}g(v)=0,

(5.93)

in the sense of distributions. Multiplying
\overline{g}
to (
5.93
), integrating over
\mathbb{R}
and taking the real part, we obtain that

g(0)=0
. It follows that for
v\in\mathbb{R}
,

(k^{2}-\partial_{v}^{2})g(v)+\frac{8e^{2v}}{e^{2v}-1}g(v)=0,

(5.94)

in the sense of distributions. Noting that
\frac{8e^{2v}}{e^{2v}-1}>0
for
v>0
and that
g(0)=0
, by the maximum principle, we conclude that
g\equiv 0
for
v\geq 0
. Then by simple ODE argument and Gronwall type inequalities, we conclude that
g\equiv 0
on
\mathbb{R}
, a contradiction with (
5.90
). The proof of (
5.79
) is then complete. The proof of (
5.80
) follows the same line of argument, but is simpler since we no longer need to renormalize the sequence as in (
5.89
).
∎

6.
The explicitly solvable case:
|k|=1

We first consider the special case when
|k|=1
, which is, remarkably, explicitly solvable as observed in
[
5
]
. Since we focus on the spectral density functions rather than the explicit formula for
f_{k}(t,v)
studied in
[
5
]
, we provide some details of the calculations. Theorem
1.4
for the case
|k|=1
follows from the following explicit formula.

Proposition 6.1
.

Assume that
k\in\{1,-1\}
. For
v,w\in\mathbb{R}
, we have

\begin{split}\Gamma_{k}(v,w)&=2\lim_{\epsilon\to 0+}\Im\,\Gamma^{+}_{k,\epsilon}(v,w)=2\lim_{\epsilon\to 0+}\Im\,\Pi_{k,\epsilon}^{+}(v,w)\\
&=2\pi\frac{B(v)-B(w)}{(\partial_{w}B(w))^{2}}e^{v+w}\mathbf{1}_{v<w}\bigg\{f_{0}^{k}(w)-e^{-w}\frac{D(w)}{\partial_{w}B(w)}\int_{-\infty}^{w}f_{0}^{k}(\rho)e^{3\rho}\,d\rho\bigg\}.\end{split}

(6.1)

The rest of the section is devoted to the proof of proposition
6.1
.

We note first that for
\epsilon\in(0,1/8)
, the function

h_{w,\epsilon}(v):=\big(B(v)-B(w)+i\epsilon\big)e^{v}

(6.2)

for
v\in\mathbb{R}
solves

(1-\partial_{v}^{2})h_{w,\epsilon}(v)+\frac{e^{2v}D(v)}{B(v)-B(w)+i\epsilon}h_{w,\epsilon}(v)=0,\qquad{\rm on}\,\,\mathbb{R}.

(6.3)

By the general theory of ODEs, we can find another solution to (
6.3
)

g_{w,\epsilon}(v):=h_{w,\epsilon}(v)\int_{v}^{\infty}\frac{d\sigma}{h^{2}_{w,\epsilon}(\sigma)}.

(6.4)

Define for
\epsilon\in(0,1/4),w\in\mathbb{R},v,\rho\in\mathbb{R}
,

G_{w,\epsilon}(v,\rho):=h_{w,\epsilon}(v)g_{w,\epsilon}(\rho)\mathbf{1}_{(-\infty,\rho)}(v)+g_{w,\epsilon}(v)h_{w,\epsilon}(\rho)\mathbf{1}_{[\rho,\infty)}(v).

(6.5)

Direct calculations show that
G_{w,\epsilon}(v,\rho)
is the fundamental solution to the differential operator

1-\partial_{v}^{2}+\frac{e^{2v}D(v)}{B(v)-B(w)+i\epsilon},\qquad{\rm on}\,\,\mathbb{R}.

(6.6)

Therefore for
\epsilon\in(0,1/4),w,v,\rho\in\mathbb{R}
, we have

\Pi_{k,\epsilon}^{+}(v,w)=\int_{\mathbb{R}}G_{w,\epsilon}(v,\rho)\frac{e^{2\rho}f_{0}^{k}(\rho)}{B(\rho)-B(w)+i\epsilon}\,d\rho.

(6.7)

Define
\epsilon\in(0,1/4),w\in\mathbb{R},v\in\mathbb{R}
,

W_{w,\epsilon}(v):=\int_{v}^{\infty}\frac{d\sigma}{h^{2}_{w,\epsilon}(\sigma)}.

(6.8)

Simple computation shows

\sup_{\rho\in[w+1,\infty)}\big|W_{w,\epsilon}(\rho)\big|\lesssim_{w}1,\quad\sup_{\rho\in[w+1,\infty)}\big|\Im W_{w,\epsilon}(\rho)\big|\lesssim\epsilon.

(6.9)

For
\rho\in(-\infty,w+1]
, we have

\begin{split}W_{w,\epsilon}(\rho)&=\int_{\rho}^{\infty}\frac{e^{-2\sigma}}{(B(\sigma)-B(w)+i\epsilon)^{2}}d\sigma\\
&=\int_{\rho}^{w+1}-\frac{e^{-2\sigma}}{\partial_{\sigma}B(\sigma)}\partial_{\sigma}\Big[\frac{1}{B(\sigma)-B(w)+i\epsilon}\Big]d\sigma+\int_{w+1}^{\infty}\frac{e^{-2\sigma}}{(B(\sigma)-B(w)+i\epsilon)^{2}}d\sigma\\
&=\frac{e^{-2\rho}}{\partial_{\rho}B(\rho)(B(\rho)-B(w)+i\epsilon)}-\frac{e^{-2(w+1)}}{\partial_{w}B(w+1)(B(w+1)-B(w)+i\epsilon)}\\
&\quad+\int_{\rho}^{w+1}\partial_{\sigma}\Big[\frac{e^{-2\sigma}}{\partial_{\sigma}B(\sigma)}\Big]\frac{1}{B(\sigma)-B(w)+i\epsilon}d\sigma+\int_{w+1}^{\infty}\frac{e^{-2\sigma}}{(B(\sigma)-B(w)+i\epsilon)^{2}}d\sigma,\end{split}

(6.10)

It follows from (
6.10
) that

|\epsilon\,W_{w,\epsilon}(\rho)|\lesssim_{w}e^{4|\rho|},\quad{\rm and}\quad\lim_{\epsilon\to 0+}\epsilon\,W_{w,\epsilon}(\rho)=0,\,\,{\rm for}\,\,\rho\in(-\infty,w+1]\backslash\{w\},

(6.11)

and in the sense of distributions for
\rho\in(-\infty,w+1)
,

\lim_{\epsilon\to 0+}\Im\,W_{w,\epsilon}(\rho)=\pi\frac{e^{-2w}}{(\partial_{w}B(w))^{2}}\delta(\rho-w)+\pi\frac{1}{\partial_{w}B(w)}\partial_{w}\Big[\frac{e^{-2w}}{\partial_{w}B(w)}\Big]{\bf 1}_{\rho<w}.

(6.12)

Applying the limiting absorption principle to the equations (
5.2
) and (
5.3
), see Lemmas
5.2
and
5.6
, we can obtain for
\Gamma_{1,\epsilon}^{\iota}(v,w)
for
\iota\in\{+,-\},w\in\mathbb{R}
and
\epsilon\in\mathbb{R}
with
0<|\epsilon|<\epsilon_{\ast}e^{-2|w|}
, the bounds

\sup_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big[\|e^{|v|}\Gamma_{k,\epsilon}^{\iota}(v,w)\|_{L^{2}(j,j+2)}+\|e^{|v|}\partial_{v}\Gamma_{k,\epsilon}^{\iota}(v,w)\|_{L^{2}(j,j+2)}\Big]\lesssim_{w}1.

(6.13)

The bounds (
6.13
) imply that to calculate the limit (
6.1
) it suffices to consider the case
v\neq w
.

We can calculate, using (
6.7
), that for
v,w\in\mathbb{R}
with
v\neq w
,

\begin{split}\lim_{\epsilon\to 0+}\Im\,\Gamma^{+}_{k,\epsilon}(v,w)&=\lim_{\epsilon\to 0+}\Im\,\Pi^{+}_{k,\epsilon}(v,w)\\
&=\lim_{\epsilon\to 0+}\Im\int_{v}^{\infty}(B(v)-B(w)-i\epsilon)e^{v+\rho}W_{w,\epsilon}(\rho)f_{0}^{k}(\rho)e^{2\rho}\,d\rho\\
&\qquad+\lim_{\epsilon\to 0+}\Im\int_{-\infty}^{v}(B(v)-B(w)+i\epsilon)e^{v+\rho}W_{w,\epsilon}(v)f_{0}^{k}(\rho)e^{2\rho}\,d\rho\\
&=\lim_{\epsilon\to 0+}\Im\int_{v}^{\infty}(B(v)-B(w))e^{v+\rho}W_{w,\epsilon}(\rho)f_{0}^{k}(\rho)e^{2\rho}\,d\rho\\
&\qquad+\lim_{\epsilon\to 0+}\Im\int_{-\infty}^{v}(B(v)-B(w))e^{v+\rho}W_{w,\epsilon}(v)f_{0}^{k}(\rho)e^{2\rho}\,d\rho.\end{split}

(6.14)

Therefore by (
6.9
) and (
6.11
)-(
6.12
), we obtain that

\begin{split}&\lim_{\epsilon\to 0+}\Im\,\Gamma^{+}_{k,\epsilon}(v,w)\\
&=\pi\frac{B(v)-B(w)}{(\partial_{w}B(w))^{2}}e^{v+w}\mathbf{1}_{v<w}f_{0}^{k}(w)+\pi\mathbf{1}_{v<w}\int_{v}^{w}\frac{e^{v}(B(v)-B(w))}{\partial_{w}B(w)}\partial_{w}\Big[\frac{e^{-2w}}{\partial_{w}B(w)}\Big]f_{0}^{k}(\rho)e^{3\rho}\,d\rho\\
&\quad+\pi\mathbf{1}_{v<w}\int_{-\infty}^{v}\frac{e^{v}(B(v)-B(w))}{\partial_{w}B(w)}\partial_{w}\Big[\frac{e^{-2w}}{\partial_{w}B(w)}\Big]f_{0}^{k}(\rho)e^{3\rho}\,d\rho.\end{split}

(6.15)

The desired formula (
6.1
) follows from the identity for
w\in\mathbb{R}
,

2\partial_{w}B(w)+\partial_{w}^{2}B(w)=e^{2w}D(w).

(6.16)

7.
Bounds on the spectral density function I: preliminary bounds

In this section we obtain important bounds on the spectral density functions
\Pi_{k,\epsilon}^{\iota}(v,w)
with
k\in\mathbb{Z}\backslash\{0\},\iota\in\{+,-\},\epsilon\in[-1/8,1/8]\backslash\{0\}
and
v,w\in\mathbb{R}
, with a focus on capturing the optimal decay property. We first establish bounds in low regularity Sobolev spaces, which are then used as a stepping stone to obtain stronger bounds in Gevrey spaces. The main tools are the limiting absorption principle proved in section
5
, and commutator type arguments.

For applications below, we fix smooth cutoff functions
\Psi,\Psi^{\ast},\Psi^{\ast\ast}:\mathbb{R}\to[0,1]
with
\Psi\in C^{\infty}(-2,2)
satisfying
\Psi\equiv 1
on
[-1,1]
,
\Psi^{\ast}\in C^{\infty}_{0}(-4,4)
satisfying
\Psi^{\ast}\equiv 1
on
[-3,3]
, and
\Psi^{\ast\ast}\in C^{\infty}_{0}(-5,5)
satisfying
\Psi^{\ast\ast}\equiv 1
on
[-9/2,9/2]
. Arrange in addition for
h\in\{\Psi,\Psi^{\ast},\Psi^{\ast\ast}\}
, that

\sup_{\xi\in\mathbb{R}}\big|e^{\langle\xi\rangle^{5/6}}\widehat{\,h\,}(\xi)\big|\lesssim 1
.

7.1.
The bounds for
\Pi_{k,\epsilon}^{\iota}(v,w)
for
|k|\geq 2

Recall that the spectral density function
\Pi_{k,\epsilon}^{\iota}(v,w)
with
k\in\mathbb{Z}\backslash\{0\},\iota\in\{+,-\},\epsilon\in[-1/8,1/8]\backslash\{0\}
and
v,w\in\mathbb{R}
, satisfy for
v\in\mathbb{R},w\in\mathbb{R}
,

(k^{2}-\partial_{v}^{2})\Pi_{k,\epsilon}^{\iota}(v,w)+\frac{e^{2v}D(v)}{B(v)-B(w)+i\iota\epsilon}\Pi_{k,\epsilon}^{\iota}(v,w)=\frac{e^{2v}f_{0}^{k}(v)}{B(v)-B(w)+i\iota\epsilon}.

(7.1)

Our goal is to use the limiting absorption principle to obtain bounds on the spectral density function
\Pi_{k,\epsilon}^{\iota}(v,w)
. Recall that
\Phi_{0}(v)\in C_{0}^{\infty}(-\infty,-1)
with
\Phi_{0}\equiv 1
on
(-\infty,-2]
and
\sup_{\xi\in\mathbb{R}}\big|e^{\langle\xi\rangle^{8/9}}\widehat{\partial_{v}\Phi_{0}}(\xi)\big|\lesssim 1
, and

\Pi_{k,\epsilon}^{\iota}(v,w)=(\sigma_{k}/c_{\ast})e^{|k|v}\Phi_{0}(v)+\Gamma_{k,\epsilon}^{\iota}(v,w).

(7.2)

It follows from (
7.1
) that
\Gamma_{k,\epsilon}^{\iota}(v,w)
satisfies the equation

\begin{split}&(k^{2}-\partial_{v}^{2})\Gamma_{k,\epsilon}^{\iota}(v,w)+\frac{e^{2v}D(v)}{B(v)-B(w)+i\iota\epsilon}\Gamma_{k,\epsilon}^{\iota}(v,w)\\
&=\frac{e^{2v}F_{0k}(v)}{B(v)-B(w)+i\iota\epsilon}+(\sigma_{k}/c_{\ast})\big(2|k|e^{|k|v}\partial_{v}\Phi_{0}(v)+e^{|k|v}\partial_{v}^{2}\Phi_{0}(v)\big).\end{split}

(7.3)

Lemma 7.1
.

There exists
\epsilon_{\ast}\in(0,1)
sufficiently small such that the following statement holds. Assume that
k\in\mathbb{Z}\backslash\{0\}
with
|k|\geq 2
,
\iota\in\{+,-\}
,
w_{\ast}\in\mathbb{R}
, and
\epsilon\in\mathbb{R}
with
0<|\epsilon|<\epsilon_{\ast}e^{-2|w_{\ast}|}
. Recall the definition (
5.58
) for
\varrho_{k,w}(v),v\in\mathbb{R}
. Then the spectral density function
\Gamma_{k,\epsilon}^{\iota}(v,w)
satisfies for
w_{\ast}\leq-15

\begin{split}&\Big\|\sup_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big[\big\|\varrho_{\varkappa_{k},w_{\ast}}(v)\Gamma_{k,\epsilon}^{\iota}(v,w)\Psi(w-w_{\ast})\big\|_{L^{2}(v\in[j,j+2])}\\
&\qquad+|k|^{-1}\big\|\varrho_{\varkappa_{k},w_{\ast}}(v)\partial_{v}\Gamma_{k,\epsilon}^{\iota}(v,w)\Psi(w-w_{\ast})\big\|_{L^{2}(v\in[j,j+2])}\Big]\Big\|_{L^{2}(w\in\mathbb{R})}\lesssim(M_{k}^{\dagger}+|\sigma_{k}|)/|k|;\end{split}

(7.4)

and for
w_{\ast}\geq-15
,

\begin{split}&\Big\|\sup_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big[\big\|e^{\varkappa_{k}|v|}\Gamma_{k,\epsilon}^{\iota}(v,w)\Psi(w-w_{\ast})\big\|_{L^{2}(v\in[j,j+2])}\\
&\qquad+|k|^{-1}\big\|e^{\varkappa_{k}|v|}\partial_{v}\Gamma_{k,\epsilon}^{\iota}(v,w)\Psi(w-w_{\ast})\big\|_{L^{2}(v\in[j,j+2])}\Big]\Big\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}\lesssim(M_{k}^{\dagger}+|\sigma_{k}|)/|k|.\end{split}

(7.5)

Proof.

We can assume
k\geq 2
and
M_{k}^{\dagger}+|\sigma_{k}|=1
without loss of generality. For
w_{\ast}\leq-15
, we can reformulate the equation
7.3
as

\begin{split}&\Gamma_{k,\epsilon}^{\iota}(v,w)+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{G}_{k}^{w}(v,\rho)\Big[\frac{e^{2\rho}D(\rho)}{B(\rho)-B(w)+i\iota\epsilon}-V_{w}(\rho)\Big]\Gamma_{k,\epsilon}^{\iota}(\rho,w)\,d\rho\\
&=\mathcal{R}_{1}(v,w):=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{G}_{k}^{w}(v,\rho)\bigg[\frac{e^{2\rho}F_{0k}(\rho)}{B(\rho)-B(w)+i\iota\epsilon}+\frac{\sigma_{k}}{c_{\ast}}\big(ke^{k\rho}\partial_{\rho}\Phi_{0}(\rho)+e^{k\rho}\partial_{\rho}^{2}\Phi_{0}(\rho)\big)\bigg]\,d\rho.\end{split}

(7.6)

We have the bounds (recalling the definitions (
5.56
)-(
5.57
) for
Y_{k,k_{\ast},w}
)

\big\|\|\mathcal{R}_{1}(v,w)\Psi(w-w_{\ast})\|_{Y_{k,\varkappa_{k},w_{\ast}}}\big\|_{L^{2}(w\in\mathbb{R})}\lesssim 1/|k|.

(7.7)

The proof of (
7.7
) is standard, using the boundedness of Hilbert transforms, and is also subsumed in the proof of the stronger bounds (
7.56
)-(
7.57
) below. We omit the repeated details and refer to the proof of (
7.56
)-(
7.57
) for details. The desired bounds (
7.4
) then follow from the limiting absorption principle, see Lemma
5.6
, applied for each
w
with
|w-w_{\ast}|\leq 2
and then square integrate in
w
.

For
w\geq-15
, we can reformulate the equation
7.3
as

\begin{split}&\Gamma_{k,\epsilon}^{\iota}(v,w)+\frac{1}{2|k|}\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{-|k(v-\rho)|}\frac{e^{2\rho}D(\rho)}{B(\rho)-B(w)+i\iota\epsilon}\Gamma_{k,\epsilon}^{\iota}(\rho,w)\,d\rho\\
&=\mathcal{R}_{2}(v,w):=\frac{1}{2|k|}\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{-|k(v-\rho)|}\bigg[\frac{e^{2\rho}F_{0k}(\rho)}{B(\rho)-B(w)+i\iota\epsilon}+\frac{\sigma_{k}}{c_{\ast}}\big(2ke^{k\rho}\partial_{\rho}\Phi_{0}(\rho)+e^{k\rho}\partial_{\rho}^{2}\Phi_{0}(\rho)\big)\bigg]\,d\rho.\end{split}

(7.8)

We have the bounds (see (
5.48
)-(
5.50
) for the definition of the space
Y_{k,\varkappa_{k}}
)

\Big\|\big\|\mathcal{R}_{2}(v,w)\Psi(w-w_{\ast})\big\|_{Y_{k,\varkappa_{k}}}\Big\|_{L^{2}(w\in\mathbb{R})}\lesssim 1/|k|.

(7.9)

The proof of (
7.9
) follows from standard calculations and the boundedness property of Hilbert transforms, and is subsumed in the proof of the stronger bounds (
7.27
)-(
7.31
). We omit the repeated details and refer to the proof of (
7.27
)-(
7.31
).

The desired bounds (
7.5
) then follow from the limiting absorption principle, see Lemma
5.2
, applied for each
w
with
|w-w_{\ast}|\leq 2
and square integrate in
w
.
∎

Assume that
k\in\mathbb{Z}\backslash\{0\}
with
|k|\geq 2
,
\iota\in\{+,-\}
,
w_{\ast}\in\mathbb{R}
, and
\epsilon\in\mathbb{R}
with
0<\epsilon<\epsilon_{\ast}\,e^{-2|w_{\ast}|}
for sufficiently small
\epsilon_{\ast}>0
from Lemma
7.1
. We now turn to the Gevrey estimates of
\Gamma_{k,\epsilon}^{\iota}
.

7.1.1.
The case
w\geq-15

We first consider the the case
w\geq-15
. For fixed
w_{\ast}\in[-10,\infty)
, we define for
v,w\in\mathbb{R}
,

\Theta_{k,\epsilon}^{\iota,w_{\ast}}(v,w):=\Psi(w-w_{\ast})\Gamma_{k,\epsilon}^{\iota}(v+w,w).

(7.10)

Define the Fourier multiplier operator
A_{k}
as

\widehat{A_{k}h}(\xi):=e^{\delta_{1}\langle k,\xi\rangle^{1/2}}\widehat{\,\,h\,\,}(\xi),\qquad{\rm for\,\,any\,\,}\xi\in\mathbb{R},h\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}),

(7.11)

and the norm for functions
h:\mathbb{R}^{2}\to\mathbb{C}
with
h\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
,

\begin{split}\|h\|_{Z}:=\Big\|\sup_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big[&\big\|e^{\varkappa_{k}|v+w_{\ast}|}A_{k}\big[\Theta_{k,\epsilon}^{\iota,w_{\ast}}(v,\cdot)\big](w)\big\|_{L^{2}(v\in[j,j+2])}\\
&+|k|^{-1}\big\|e^{\varkappa_{k}|v+w_{\ast}|}\partial_{v}A_{k}\big[\Theta_{k,\epsilon}^{\iota,w_{\ast}}(v,\cdot)\big](w)\big\|_{L^{2}(v\in[j,j+2])}\Big]\Big\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}.\end{split}

(7.12)

For notational conveniences, we also introduce the shifted
Y_{k}
norm for
h:\mathbb{R}^{2}\to\mathbb{C}
,

\begin{split}\|h\|_{Y_{\varkappa_{k}}^{+w_{\ast}}}:=\Big\|\sup_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big[&\big\|e^{\varkappa_{k}|v+w_{\ast}|}h(v,w)\big\|_{L^{2}(v\in[j,j+2])}\\
&+|k|^{-1}\big\|e^{\varkappa_{k}|v+w_{\ast}|}\partial_{v}h(v,w)\big\|_{L^{2}(v\in[j,j+2])}\Big]\Big\|_{L^{2}(w\in\mathbb{R})}.\end{split}

(7.13)

Then

\big\|h(v,w)\big\|_{Z}=\big\|A_{k}\big[h(v,\cdot)\big](w)\big\|_{Y_{\varkappa_{k}}^{+w_{\ast}}}.

(7.14)

We have the following result.

Proposition 7.2
.

Assume that
k\in\mathbb{Z}\backslash\{0\}
with
|k|\geq 2
,
w_{\ast}\in[-15,\infty)
,
\iota\in\{+,-\}
and
\epsilon\in\mathbb{R}
with
0<\epsilon<e^{-2|w_{\ast}|}\epsilon_{\ast}
. We have the bounds

\begin{split}&\sup_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\big\|e^{\varkappa_{k}|v+w_{\ast}|}\big[\big|A_{k}\big[\Theta_{k,\epsilon}^{\iota,w_{\ast}}(v,\cdot)\big](w)\big|+|k|^{-1}\big|\partial_{v}A_{k}\big[\Theta_{k,\epsilon}^{\iota,w_{\ast}}(v,\cdot)\big](w)\big|\big]\big\|_{L^{2}(v\in[j,j+2],w\in\mathbb{R})}\\
&\lesssim(M_{k}^{\dagger}+|\sigma_{k}|)/|k|.\end{split}

(7.15)

Proof.

We can assume, without loss of generality,
k\geq 2
and
M_{k}^{\dagger}+|\sigma_{k}|=1
. By lemma
7.1
we have

\big\|\Theta_{k,\epsilon}^{\iota,w_{\ast}}(v,w)\big\|_{Y_{\varkappa_{k}}^{+w_{\ast}}}\lesssim 1/|k|,

(7.16)

which we will use to control the low frequency part of the desired bounds (
7.15
).

Denote

M:=\sup_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\big\|e^{\varkappa_{k}|v+w_{\ast}|}\big[\big|A_{k}\big[\Theta_{k,\epsilon}^{\iota,w_{\ast}}(v,\cdot)\big](w)\big|+|k|^{-1}\big|\partial_{v}A_{k}\big[\Theta_{k,\epsilon}^{\iota,w_{\ast}}(v,\cdot)\big](w)\big|\big]\big\|_{L^{2}(v\in[j,j+2],w\in\mathbb{R})}.

We need to prove that
M\lesssim 1/|k|
. From the equation (
7.8
), we obtain that for
v,w\in\mathbb{R}
,

\begin{split}&\Theta_{k,\epsilon}^{\iota,w_{\ast}}(v,w)+\frac{1}{2|k|}\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{-|k||v-\rho|}\frac{e^{2\rho+2w}D(\rho+w)}{B(\rho+w)-B(w)+i\iota\epsilon}\Theta_{k,\epsilon}^{\iota,w_{\ast}}(\rho,w)\,d\rho\\
&=\frac{1}{2|k|}\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{-|k||v-\rho|}\frac{e^{2\rho+2w}}{B(\rho+w)-B(w)+i\iota\epsilon}h_{0k}^{1}(\rho,w)\,d\rho+\frac{1}{2|k|}\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{-|k||v-\rho|}h_{0k}^{2}(\rho,w)\,d\rho\\
&:=H_{1k}(v,w)+H_{2k}(v,w).\end{split}

(7.17)

where for
\rho,w\in\mathbb{R}
, (recall that
\sigma_{k}=0
for
|k|\geq k^{\dagger}
)

\begin{split}&h_{0k}^{1}(\rho,w)=F_{0k}(\rho+w)\Psi(w-w_{\ast}),\\
&h_{0k}^{2}(\rho,w)=\frac{\sigma_{k}}{c_{\ast}}e^{|k|(\rho+w)}\big(2|k|\partial_{\rho}\Phi_{0}(\rho+w)+\partial_{\rho}^{2}\Phi_{0}(\rho+w)\big)\Psi(w-w_{\ast}).\end{split}

(7.18)

Applying the Fourier multiplier operator
A_{k}
(in the variable
w
) to (
7.17
) and multiplying the cutoff function
\Psi^{\ast}(w-w_{\ast})
, we obtain

\begin{split}&\Psi^{\ast}(w-w_{\ast})A_{k}\big[\Theta_{k,\epsilon}^{\iota,w_{\ast}}(v,\cdot)\big](w)+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{e^{-|k||v-\rho|}e^{2\rho+2w}D(\rho+w)\Psi^{\ast}(w-w_{\ast})}{2|k|(B(\rho+w)-B(w)+i\iota\epsilon)}A_{k}\big[\Theta_{k,\epsilon}^{\iota,w_{\ast}}(\rho,\cdot)\big](w)\,d\rho\\
&=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{e^{-|k||v-\rho|}}{2|k|}e^{2\rho+2w_{\ast}}\frac{\Psi^{\ast}(w-w_{\ast})}{B^{\prime}(w_{\ast})}\Big\{K(\rho,w)A_{k}\big[\Theta_{k,\epsilon}^{\iota,w_{\ast}}(\rho,\cdot)\big](w)-A_{k}\big[K(\rho,\cdot)\Theta_{k,\epsilon}^{\iota,w_{\ast}}(\rho,\cdot)\big](w)\Big\}\,d\rho\\
&\quad+\Psi^{\ast}(w-w_{\ast})A_{k}\Big[H_{1k}(v,\cdot)\Big](w)+\Psi^{\ast}(w-w_{\ast})A_{k}\Big[H_{2k}(v,\cdot)\Big](w)\\
&:=\mathcal{C}_{k}(v,w)+\Psi^{\ast}(w-w_{\ast})A_{k}\Big[H_{1k}(v,\cdot)\Big](w)+\Psi^{\ast}(w-w_{\ast})A_{k}\Big[H_{2k}(v,\cdot)\Big](w).\end{split}

(7.19)

where we set for
\rho,w\in\mathbb{R}
,

K(\rho,w):=\frac{e^{2(w-w_{\ast})}D(\rho+w)B^{\prime}(w_{\ast})}{B(\rho+w)-B(w)+i\iota\epsilon}\Psi^{\ast\ast}(w-w_{\ast}),\qquad K^{\ast}(\rho,w):=\Psi^{\ast}(\rho)K(\rho,w).

(7.20)

By the definitions (
7.18
), (
7.20
) and the assumption
1.3
, (denoting
\widehat{\,\varphi\,}(\rho,\xi)
as the Fourier transform of
\varphi(\rho,w)
in
w
), in view of lemma
2.3
, we have the following bounds for
\rho\in\mathbb{R}
and
w_{\ast}\in[-15,\infty)
,

\begin{split}&\big\|e^{\delta_{1}\langle k,\xi\rangle^{1/2}}\widehat{\,h_{0k}^{1}}(\rho,\xi)\big\|_{L^{2}(\xi\in\mathbb{R})}\lesssim\frac{e^{\mu^{\ast}_{\varkappa_{k}}(\rho+w_{\ast})}}{1+e^{(\mu^{\ast}_{\varkappa_{k}}+\varkappa_{k}+8)(\rho+w_{\ast})}},\\
&\big\|e^{\langle k,\xi\rangle^{4/5}}\widehat{\,h_{0k}^{2}}(\rho,\xi)\big\|_{L^{2}(\xi\in\mathbb{R})}\lesssim\mathbf{1}_{[-w_{\ast}-8,-w_{\ast}+4]}(\rho),\\
&\big\|e^{\delta_{0}\langle\xi\rangle^{1/2}}\widehat{\,K\,}(\rho,\xi)\big\|_{L^{2}(\xi\in\mathbb{R})}\lesssim\frac{1}{1+e^{8(\rho+w_{\ast})}}\Big|\frac{B^{\prime}(w_{\ast})}{B(\rho+w_{\ast})-B(w_{\ast})+i\epsilon}\Big|,\quad{\rm for}\,\,|\rho|\geq 1/2,\\
&\sup_{\eta\in\mathbb{R}}\big\|e^{\delta_{0}\langle\xi\rangle^{1/2}}\widehat{\,K^{\ast}}(\eta,\xi)\big\|_{L^{2}(\xi\in\mathbb{R})}\lesssim\frac{1}{1+e^{8w_{\ast}}}.\end{split}

(7.21)

To apply the limiting absorption principle, see lemma
5.2
, we need to bound the terms in the last line of (
7.19
) and prove that for
h\in\big\{\mathcal{C}_{k}(v,w),A_{k}\big[H_{1k}(v,\cdot)\big](w),A_{k}\big[H_{2k}(v,\cdot)\big](w)\big\}
,

\big\|h\big\|_{Y_{k}^{+w_{\ast}}}\lesssim(C_{\gamma}+\gamma M)/|k|,

(7.22)

for sufficiently small
\gamma>0
and suitable
C_{\gamma}\in(0,\infty)
.
We first bound the term
A_{k}\big[H_{1k}(v,\cdot)\big](w)
. Set for
v\in\mathbb{R}
,

F_{0k}^{w_{\ast}}(v):=\Psi^{\ast\ast}(v-w_{\ast})F_{0k}(v),

(7.23)

and

\begin{split}h_{0k}^{1\ast}(\rho,w)&:=\Psi(\rho)\frac{e^{2w-2w_{\ast}}B^{\prime}(w_{\ast})}{B(\rho+w)-B(w)+i\iota\epsilon}h_{0k}^{1}(\rho,w)\Psi^{\ast}(w-w_{\ast})\\
&=\Psi(\rho)F_{0k}^{w_{\ast}}(\rho+w)\frac{e^{2w-2w_{\ast}}B^{\prime}(w_{\ast})}{B(\rho+w)-B(w)+i\iota\epsilon}\Psi^{\ast}(w-w_{\ast}).\end{split}

(7.24)

it follows from (
7.18
) and lemma
2.3
that

\begin{split}&\Big\|\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{-(\delta_{0}/2)\langle\alpha\rangle^{1/2}}\big|A_{k}\big[h_{0k}^{1}(\rho,\cdot)e^{i\alpha\cdot}\big](w)\big|\,d\alpha\Big\|_{L^{2}(w\in\mathbb{R})}\\
&\lesssim\frac{e^{\mu^{\ast}_{\varkappa_{k}}(\rho+w_{\ast})}}{1+e^{(\mu^{\ast}_{\varkappa_{k}}+\varkappa_{k}+8)(\rho+w_{\ast})}},\end{split}

(7.25)

and for
\eta\in\mathbb{R}
,

\begin{split}&\Big\|\int_{\mathbb{R}}A_{k}\big[h_{0k}^{1\ast}(\rho,\cdot)\big](w)e^{-i\rho\eta}\,d\rho\Big\|_{L^{2}(w\in\mathbb{R})}\\
&\lesssim\Big\|\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{-\delta_{0}\langle\alpha\rangle^{1/2}}e^{\delta_{1}\langle k,\xi\rangle^{1/2}}\big|\widehat{\,F_{0k}^{w_{\ast}}}(\xi-\alpha)\big|\,d\alpha\Big\|_{L^{2}(\xi\in\mathbb{R})}\lesssim\frac{e^{\mu^{\ast}_{\varkappa_{k}}w_{\ast}}}{1+e^{(\mu^{\ast}_{\varkappa_{k}}+\varkappa_{k}+8)w_{\ast}}}.\end{split}

(7.26)

We can then bound
A_{k}\big[H_{1k}(v,\cdot)\big](w)
as follows. For
w\in\mathbb{R}
, by lemma
2.3
, the bounds (
7.26
) and Parseval’s identity, we have

\begin{split}&\sup_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big[e^{\varkappa_{k}|w_{\ast}+j|}\big\|\Psi^{\ast}(v-j)A_{k}\big[H_{1k}(v,\cdot)\big](w)\big\|_{L^{2}(v\in\mathbb{R})}\Big]\\
&\lesssim\sup_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big\|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\Psi^{\ast}(v-j)e^{\varkappa_{k}|w_{\ast}+\rho|}\frac{e^{2\rho+2w_{\ast}}(1-\Psi(\rho))e^{-\delta_{0}\langle\alpha\rangle^{1/2}}}{|k|\big|B(\rho+w_{\ast})-B(w_{\ast})+i\iota\epsilon\big|}\big|A_{k}\big[h^{1}_{0k}(\rho,\cdot)e^{i\alpha\cdot}\big](w)\big|\,d\rho d\alpha\Big\|_{L^{2}(v\in\mathbb{R})}\\
&+\sup_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big\|e^{\varkappa_{k}|w_{\ast}+j|}\Psi^{\ast}(v-j)\frac{1}{2k}\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{-k|v-\rho|}\frac{e^{2\rho+2w_{\ast}}\Psi^{\ast}(\rho)}{B^{\prime}(w_{\ast})}A_{k}\big[h_{0k}^{1\ast}(\rho,\cdot)\big](w)\,d\rho\Big\|_{L^{2}(v\in\mathbb{R})}\\
&\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}e^{\varkappa_{k}|w_{\ast}+\rho|}\frac{e^{2\rho+2w_{\ast}}(1-\Psi(\rho))e^{-\delta_{0}\langle\alpha\rangle^{1/2}}}{|k|\big|B(\rho+w_{\ast})-B(w_{\ast})+i\iota\epsilon\big|}\big|A_{k}\big[h^{1}_{0k}(\rho,\cdot)e^{i\alpha\cdot}\big](w)\big|\,d\rho d\alpha\\
&\qquad+e^{(\varkappa_{k}+4)w_{\ast}}\Big\|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\langle k,\eta\rangle^{-2}e^{-\delta_{0}\langle\eta+\beta\rangle^{3/4}}\Big|\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{i\beta\rho}A_{k}\big[h_{0k}^{1\ast}(\rho,\cdot)\big](w)\,d\rho\Big|d\beta\Big\|_{L^{2}(\eta\in\mathbb{R})}.\end{split}

(7.27)

In view of (
7.25
)-(
7.26
), square integrating (
7.27
) in
w\in\mathbb{R}
, we obtain that

\Big\|\sup_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\big\|e^{|k||v+w_{\ast}|}A_{k}\big[H_{1k}(v,\cdot)\big](w)\big\|_{L^{2}(v\in[j,j+2])}\Big\|_{L^{2}(w\in\mathbb{R})}\lesssim|k|^{-1}.

(7.28)

In the above, we have used the inequalities for
\alpha,\beta\in\mathbb{R}
,

\begin{split}\frac{1}{|k|}\Big|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}e^{-k|v-\rho|}\Psi^{\ast}(v-j)e^{2\rho}\Psi^{\ast}(\rho)e^{-iv\alpha-i\rho\beta}\,dvd\rho\Big|\lesssim e^{-\varkappa_{k}|j|}\langle k,\alpha\rangle^{-2}e^{-\langle\alpha+\beta\rangle^{3/4}}.\end{split}

(7.29)

Similarly we have

|k|^{-1}\Big\|\sup_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\big\|e^{|k||v+w_{\ast}|}\partial_{v}A_{k}\big[H_{1k}(v,\cdot)\big](w)\big\|_{L^{2}(v\in[j,j+2])}\Big\|_{L^{2}(w\in\mathbb{R})}\lesssim|k|^{-1}.

(7.30)

(
7.28
)-(
7.30
) imply the desired bounds (
7.22
) for
h=A_{k}\big[H_{1k}(v,\cdot)\big](w)
.

The bounds on
A_{k}\big[H_{2k}(v,\cdot)\big](w)
is simpler, and using (
7.21
) we have

\big\|A_{k}\big[H_{2k}(v,\cdot)\big](w)\big\|_{Y_{\varkappa_{k}}^{+w_{\ast}}}\lesssim|k|^{-1}.

(7.31)

(
7.31
) imply (
7.22
) for
h=A_{k}\big[H_{2k}(v,\cdot)\big](w)
.

We now bound the main commutator term
\mathcal{C}_{k}(v,w)
. Defining for
\rho,w\in\mathbb{R}
,

N(\rho,w):=A_{k}\big[K(\rho,\cdot)\Theta_{k,\epsilon}^{\iota,w_{\ast}}(\rho,\cdot)\big](w)-K(\rho,w)A_{k}\big[\Theta_{k,\epsilon}^{\iota,w_{\ast}}(\rho,\cdot)\big](w),\quad N^{\ast}(\rho,w):=\Psi^{\ast}(\rho)N(\rho,w).

(7.32)

For the simplicity of notation below we also denote for
\rho\in\mathbb{R}
,

M(\rho):=\Big\|\langle k,\partial_{w}\rangle^{-1/2}A_{k}\big[\Theta_{k,\epsilon}^{\iota,w_{\ast}}(\rho,\cdot)\big](w)\Big\|_{L^{2}(w\in\mathbb{R})}+\frac{1}{|k|}\Big\|\langle k,\partial_{w}\rangle^{-1/2}\partial_{\rho}A_{k}\big[\Theta_{k,\epsilon}^{\iota,w_{\ast}}(\rho,\cdot)\big](w)\Big\|_{L^{2}(w\in\mathbb{R})},

(7.33)

then it follows by dividing the frequency of the variable
w
into low frequency and high frequency parts, that for any
\gamma\in(0,1)
,

\sup_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\big\|e^{\varkappa_{k}|\rho+w_{\ast}|}M(\rho)\big\|_{L^{2}(\rho\in[j,j+2])}\lesssim C_{\gamma}+\gamma M.

(7.34)

Notice that using (
7.21
), we have for
|\rho|\geq 1/2
,

\begin{split}&\big\|N(\rho,w)\big\|_{L^{2}(w\in\mathbb{R})}\lesssim\Big\|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\widehat{\,K\,}(\rho,\alpha)\Big[e^{\delta_{1}\langle k,\xi\rangle^{1/2}}-e^{\delta_{1}\langle k,\xi-\alpha\rangle^{1/2}}\Big]\widehat{\,\,\Theta_{k,\epsilon}^{\iota,w_{\ast}}}(\rho,\xi-\alpha)\,d\alpha\Big\|_{L^{2}(\xi\in\mathbb{R})}\\
&\lesssim\Big\|\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{(\delta_{0}/4)\langle\alpha\rangle^{1/2}}\big|\widehat{\,K\,}(\rho,\alpha)\big|e^{\delta_{1}\langle k,\xi-\alpha\rangle^{1/2}}\langle k,\xi-\alpha\rangle^{-1/2}\big|\widehat{\,\,\Theta_{k,\epsilon}^{\iota,w_{\ast}}}(\rho,\xi-\alpha)\big|\,d\alpha\Big\|_{L^{2}(\xi\in\mathbb{R})}\\
&\lesssim M(\rho)\frac{1}{1+e^{8(\rho+w_{\ast})}}\Big|\frac{B^{\prime}(w_{\ast})}{B(\rho+w_{\ast})-B(w_{\ast})+i\epsilon}\Big|.\end{split}

(7.35)

Denoting
Q(\rho,w):=\Psi^{\ast\ast}(\rho)\Theta_{k,\epsilon}^{\iota,w_{\ast}}(\rho,w)
for
\rho,w\in\mathbb{R}
, for the brevity of notations, we have

\begin{split}&\int_{\mathbb{R}}\big\|\langle k,\xi\rangle^{-1/2}e^{\delta_{1}\langle k,\xi\rangle^{1/2}}\widehat{\,Q\,}(\gamma,\xi)\big\|_{L^{2}(\xi\in\mathbb{R})}\,d\gamma\\
&\lesssim|k|^{1/2}\big\|\langle\gamma/k\rangle\langle k,\xi\rangle^{-1/2}e^{\delta_{1}\langle k,\xi\rangle^{1/2}}\widehat{\,Q\,}(\gamma,\xi)\big\|_{L^{2}(\gamma,\xi\in\mathbb{R})}\lesssim|k|^{1/2}\big\|\Psi^{\ast}(\rho)M(\rho)\big\|_{L^{2}(\rho\in\mathbb{R})}.\end{split}

(7.36)

Therefore, in view of by (
7.21
), (
7.32
) and (
7.36
), we have for
\eta\in\mathbb{R}
,

\begin{split}&\Big\|\int_{\mathbb{R}}N^{\ast}(\rho,w)e^{-i\rho\eta}\,d\rho\Big\|_{L^{2}(w\in\mathbb{R})}\\
&\lesssim\Big\|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\big|\widehat{\,\,K^{\ast}}(\gamma,\alpha)\big|\Big|e^{\delta_{1}\langle k,\xi\rangle^{1/2}}-e^{\delta_{1}\langle k,\xi-\alpha\rangle^{1/2}}\Big|\big|\widehat{\,Q\,}(\eta-\gamma,\xi-\alpha)\big|\,d\alpha d\gamma\Big\|_{L^{2}(\xi\in\mathbb{R})}\\
&\lesssim\Big\|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}e^{(\delta_{0}/4)\langle\alpha\rangle^{1/2}}\big|\widehat{\,\,K^{\ast}}(\gamma,\alpha)\big|e^{\delta_{1}\langle k,\xi-\alpha\rangle^{1/2}}\langle k,\xi-\alpha\rangle^{-1/2}\big|\widehat{\,Q\,}(\eta-\gamma,\xi-\alpha)\big|\,d\alpha d\gamma\Big\|_{L^{2}(\xi\in\mathbb{R})}\\
&\lesssim|k|^{1/2}\frac{1}{1+e^{8w_{\ast}}}\Big\|\Psi^{\ast\ast}(\rho)M(\rho)\Big\|_{L^{2}(\rho\in\mathbb{R})}.\end{split}

(7.37)

Using the definition (
7.19
), the bounds (
7.35
) for
|\rho|\geq 1
, together with (
7.37
) for
|\rho|\lesssim 1
, and (
7.34
), we can then bound for
w\in\mathbb{R}
and any
\gamma\in(0,1)
and suitable
C_{\gamma}\in(0,\infty)
,

\begin{split}&\Big\|\sup_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big[e^{\varkappa_{k}|j+w_{\ast}|}\big\|\Psi(v-j)\mathcal{C}_{k}(v,w)\big\|_{L^{2}(v\in\mathbb{R})}\Big]\Big\|_{L^{2}(w\in\mathbb{R})}\\
&\lesssim\frac{1}{2|k|}\bigg\|\sup_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\bigg[e^{\varkappa_{k}|j+w_{\ast}|}\Big\|\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{-|k||v-\rho|}\Psi(v-j)\frac{e^{2\rho+2w_{\ast}}}{B^{\prime}(w_{\ast})}\Psi^{\ast}(w-w_{\ast})\\
&\hskip 108.405pt\times\Big[(1-\Psi(\rho))N(\rho,w)+\Psi(\rho)N^{\ast}(\rho,w)\Big]\,d\rho\Big\|_{L^{2}(v\in\mathbb{R})}\bigg]\bigg\|_{L^{2}(w\in\mathbb{R})}\\
&\lesssim\frac{1}{2|k|}\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{\varkappa_{k}|\rho+w_{\ast}|}\frac{e^{2\rho+2w_{\ast}}}{1+e^{8(\rho+w_{\ast})}}\frac{(1-\Psi(\rho))M(\rho)}{|B(\rho+w_{\ast})-B(w_{\ast})|}\,d\rho\\
&\qquad+e^{(\varkappa_{k}+4)w_{\ast}}\Big\|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\langle k,\eta\rangle^{-2}e^{-\langle\eta+\beta\rangle^{3/4}}\Big|\int_{\mathbb{R}}N^{\ast}(\rho,w)e^{i\beta\rho}\,d\rho\Big|d\beta\Big\|_{L^{2}(\eta,w\in\mathbb{R})}\lesssim|k|^{-1}(C_{\gamma}+\gamma M).\end{split}

(7.38)

Similarly, we have for any
\gamma\in(0,1)
,

\begin{split}|k|^{-1}\Big\|\sup_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\big\|e^{\varkappa_{k}|v+w_{\ast}|}\partial_{v}\mathcal{C}_{k}(v,w)\big\|_{L^{2}(v\in[j,j+2])}\Big\|_{L^{2}(w\in\mathbb{R})}\lesssim|k|^{-1}(C_{\gamma}+\gamma M).\end{split}

(7.39)

(
7.38
)-(
7.39
), together with (
7.28
), (
7.30
)-(
7.31
) complete the proof of (
7.22
).
Therefore, by the limiting absorption principle, see lemma
5.2
with a translation in
v
by
w
, we conclude that for
\gamma\in(0,1)
and suitable
C_{\gamma}\in(0,\infty)
,

\begin{split}&\sup_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\big\|\Psi^{\ast}(w-w_{\ast})e^{\varkappa_{k}|v+w_{\ast}|}\big[\big|A_{k}\big[\Theta_{k,\epsilon}^{\iota,w_{\ast}}(v,\cdot)\big](w)\big|+|k|^{-1}\big|\partial_{v}A_{k}\big[\Theta_{k,\epsilon}^{\iota,w_{\ast}}(v,\cdot)\big](w)\big|\big]\big\|_{L^{2}(v,\in[j,j+2],w\in\mathbb{R})}\\
&\lesssim(C_{\gamma}+\gamma M)/|k|.\end{split}

(7.40)

Another simple commutator argument comparing
\Psi^{\ast}(w-w_{\ast})A_{k}\big[\Theta_{k,\epsilon}^{\iota,w_{\ast}}(v,\cdot)\big](w)
with the nonlocalized
A_{k}\big[\Theta_{k,\epsilon}^{\iota,w_{\ast}}(v,\cdot)\big](w)
shows that for
\gamma\in(0,1)
,

M\lesssim(C_{\gamma}+\gamma M)/|k|,

(7.41)

which imply the desired bounds once we choose
\gamma\in(0,1)
small enough so that the second term on the right hand side can be absorbed by the left hand side.
∎

7.1.2.
The case
w\leq-15

We now turn to the case
w\leq-15
. Assume that
w_{\ast}\leq-15
. Define the norm for functions
h:\mathbb{R}^{2}\to\mathbb{C}
with
h\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
,

\begin{split}\|h\|_{Z^{\ast}}:=\Big\|\sup_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big[&\big\|\varrho_{\varkappa_{k},w_{\ast}}(v+w_{\ast})A_{k}\big[h(v,\cdot)\big](w)\big\|_{L^{2}(v\in[j,j+2])}\\
&+|k|^{-1}\big\|\varrho_{\varkappa_{k},w_{\ast}}(v+w_{\ast})\partial_{v}A_{k}\big[h(v,\cdot)\big](w)\big\|_{L^{2}(v\in[j,j+2])}\Big]\Big\|_{L^{2}(w\in\mathbb{R})}.\end{split}

(7.42)

For notational convenience, we also introduce the translated
Y_{k,w_{\ast}}
norm for
h(v,w):\mathbb{R}^{2}\to\mathbb{C}
,

\begin{split}\|h(v,w)\|_{Y_{\varkappa_{k},w_{\ast}}^{+w_{\ast}}}:=\Big\|\sup_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big[&\big\|\varrho_{\varkappa_{k},w_{\ast}}(v+w_{\ast})h(v,w)\big\|_{L^{2}(v\in[j,j+2])}\\
&+|k|^{-1}\big\|\varrho_{\varkappa_{k},w_{\ast}}(v+w_{\ast})\partial_{v}h(v,w)\big\|_{L^{2}(v\in[j,j+2])}\Big]\Big\|_{L^{2}(w\in\mathbb{R})}.\end{split}

(7.43)

Then

\big\|h\big\|_{Z^{\ast}}=\big\|A_{k}\big[h(v,\cdot)\big](w)\big\|_{Y_{k,w_{\ast}}^{+w_{\ast}}}.

(7.44)

Our main result in this case is the following.

Proposition 7.3
.

Assume that
k\in\mathbb{Z}\backslash\{0\}
with
|k|\geq 2
,
w_{\ast}\in(-\infty,-15]
,
\iota\in\{+,-\}
,
\epsilon\in\mathbb{R}
with
0<\epsilon<e^{2w_{\ast}}\epsilon_{\ast}
. Then we have the bounds

\begin{split}&\sup_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\big\|\varrho_{\varkappa_{k},w_{\ast}}(v+w_{\ast})\big[\big|A_{k}\big[\Theta_{k,\epsilon}^{\iota,w_{\ast}}(v,\cdot)\big](w)\big|+|k|^{-1}\big|\partial_{v}A_{k}\big[\Theta_{k,\epsilon}^{\iota,w_{\ast}}(v,\cdot)\big](w)\big|\big]\big\|_{L^{2}(v\in[j,j+2],w\in\mathbb{R})}\\
&\lesssim(M_{k}^{\dagger}+|\sigma_{k}|)/|k|.\end{split}

(7.45)

Proof.

We assume, without loss of generality,
k\geq 2
and
M_{k}^{\dagger}+|\sigma_{k}|=1
. Denote

M:=\sup_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\big\|\varrho_{\varkappa_{k},w_{\ast}}(v+w_{\ast})\big[\big|A_{k}\big[\Theta_{k,\epsilon}^{\iota,w_{\ast}}(v,\cdot)\big](w)\big|+|k|^{-1}\big|\partial_{v}A_{k}\big[\Theta_{k,\epsilon}^{\iota,w_{\ast}}(v,\cdot)\big](w)\big|\big]\big\|_{L^{2}(v\in[j,j+2],w\in\mathbb{R})}.

We need to prove that
M\lesssim 1/|k|
. By lemma
7.1
, we have

\big\|\Theta_{k,\epsilon}^{\iota,w_{\ast}}(v,w)\big\|_{Y_{\varkappa_{k},w_{\ast}}^{+w_{\ast}}}\lesssim 1/|k|,

(7.46)

which will be used to control the low frequency part of the desired bounds (
7.45
).

Using the equation (
7.6
) and recalling the definitions (
7.18
) for
h_{0k}^{1},h_{0k}^{2}
, we obtain that

\begin{split}&\Theta_{k,\epsilon}^{\iota,w_{\ast}}(v,w)+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{G}_{k}^{w}(v+w,\rho+w)\Big[\frac{e^{2\rho+2w}D(\rho+w)}{B(\rho+w)-B(w)+i\iota\epsilon}-V_{w}(\rho+w)\Big]\Theta_{k,\epsilon}^{\iota,w_{\ast}}(\rho,w)\,d\rho\\
&=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{G}_{k}^{w}(v+w,\rho+w)\frac{e^{2\rho+2w}h_{0k}^{1}(\rho,w)}{B(\rho+w)-B(w)+i\iota\epsilon}\,d\rho+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{G}_{k}^{w}(v+w,\rho+w)h_{0k}^{2}(\rho,w)\,d\rho\\
&:=H_{1k}^{\ast}(v,w)+H_{2k}^{\ast}(v,w).\end{split}

(7.47)

Recall the definition (
3.36
) and define for
w_{\ast}\in\mathbb{R}
,

\mathcal{F}_{k}^{w_{\ast}}(v,\rho,w)=\mathcal{G}_{k}^{w}(v+w,\rho+w)\Psi^{\ast\ast}(w-w_{\ast}).

(7.48)

We shall use the following bounds on
\mathcal{F}_{k}^{w_{\ast}}(v,\rho,w)
, which follow from proposition
3.4
,

\begin{split}&\sup_{\xi\in\mathbb{R}}\big|e^{\delta_{0}\langle\xi\rangle^{1/2}}\widehat{\,\,\mathcal{F}_{k}^{w_{\ast}}}(v,\rho,\xi)\big|\lesssim|k|^{-1}\varpi_{\varkappa_{k},w_{\ast}}(v+w_{\ast},\rho+w_{\ast}),\quad{\rm for}\,\,v,\rho\in\mathbb{R},\\
&\Big|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\Psi^{\ast}(v-j)\Psi^{\ast}(\rho)\mathcal{F}_{k}^{w_{\ast}}(v,\rho,w)e^{-iv\alpha-i\rho\beta-iw\xi}\,dvd\rho dw\Big|\\
&\lesssim\varpi_{\varkappa_{k},w_{\ast}}(j+w_{\ast},w_{\ast})e^{-\delta_{0}\langle\xi\rangle^{1/2}}\langle k,\alpha\rangle^{-2}e^{-\delta_{0}\langle\alpha+\beta\rangle^{1/2}},\quad{\rm for}\,\,j\in\mathbb{Z},\alpha,\beta,\xi\in\mathbb{R}.\end{split}

(7.49)

Define for
\rho,w\in\mathbb{R}
,

L(\rho,w):=\Big[\frac{e^{2\rho+2w}D(\rho+w)}{B(\rho+w)-B(w)+i\iota\epsilon}-V_{w}(\rho+w)\Big]\Psi^{\ast\ast}(w-w_{\ast}),\quad L^{\ast}(\rho,w):=\Psi^{\ast}(\rho)L(\rho,w).

(7.50)

By lemma
2.3
, we have the following bounds for
L
,

\begin{split}&\sup_{\xi\in\mathbb{R}}e^{\delta_{0}\langle\xi\rangle^{1/2}}\big|\widehat{\,L\,}(\rho,\xi)\big|\lesssim e^{-2|\rho|},\,\,{\rm for}\,\,|\rho|\geq 1,\quad{\rm and}\quad\sup_{\eta,\xi\in\mathbb{R}}e^{\delta_{0}\langle\xi\rangle^{1/2}}\big|\widehat{\,L^{\ast}}(\eta,\xi)\big|\lesssim 1.\end{split}

(7.51)

Applying the Fourier multiplier operator
A_{k}
(in the variable
w
) to (
7.47
) and multiplying
\Psi^{\ast}(w-w_{\ast})
, we obtain that

\begin{split}&\Psi^{\ast}(w-w_{\ast})A_{k}\big[\Theta_{k,\epsilon}^{\iota,w_{\ast}}(v,\cdot)\big](w)\\
&+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{F}_{k}(v,\rho,w)\Big[\frac{e^{2\rho+2w}D(\rho+w)}{B(\rho+w)-B(w)+i\iota\epsilon}-V_{w}(\rho+w)\Big]\Psi^{\ast}(w-w_{\ast})A_{k}\Big[\Theta_{k,\epsilon}^{\iota,w_{\ast}}(\rho,\cdot)\Big](w)\,d\rho\\
&=\Psi^{\ast}(w-w_{\ast})A_{k}\Big[H_{1k}^{\ast}(v,\cdot)\Big](w)+\Psi^{\ast}(w-w_{\ast})A_{k}\big[H_{2k}^{\ast}(v,\cdot)\Big](w)+\Psi^{\ast}(w-w_{\ast})\mathcal{C}_{k}^{\ast}(v,w),\end{split}

(7.52)

where

\begin{split}\mathcal{C}_{k}^{\ast}(v,w):=&\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{F}_{k}^{w_{\ast}}(v,\rho,w)L(\rho,w)A_{k}\big[\Theta_{k,\epsilon}^{\iota,w_{\ast}}(\rho,\cdot)\big](w)\,d\rho\\
&-A_{k}\bigg\{\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{F}_{k}^{w_{\ast}}(v,\rho,\cdot)L(\rho,\cdot)\Theta_{k,\epsilon}^{\iota,w_{\ast}}(\rho,\cdot)\,d\rho\bigg\}(w).\end{split}

(7.53)

To apply the limiting absorption principle, see lemma
5.6
, we need to bound the terms on the right hand side of the last line of (
7.52
), and prove for
h\in\big\{A_{k}H_{1k}^{\ast}(v,w),A_{k}H_{2k}^{\ast}(v,w),\mathcal{C}_{k}^{\ast}(v,w)\big\}
,

\big\|h(v,w)\big\|_{Y_{k,w_{\ast}}^{+w_{\ast}}}\lesssim(C_{\gamma}+\gamma M)/|k|,

(7.54)

for sufficiently small
\gamma>0
and suitable
C_{\gamma}\in(0,\infty)
.

For
h=A_{k}\big[H_{1k}^{\ast}(v,\cdot)\big](w)
, using (
3.39
)-(
3.41
), (
7.25
)-(
7.26
) and lemma
2.3
, we can bound, similar to (
7.27
) that for any
w\in\mathbb{R}
,

\begin{split}&\sup_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big[\varrho_{\varkappa_{k},w_{\ast}}(j+w_{\ast})\big\|\Psi^{\ast}(v-j)A_{k}\big[H^{\ast}_{1k}(v,\cdot)\big](w)\big\|_{L^{2}(v\in\mathbb{R})}\Big]\\
&\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varrho_{\varkappa_{k},w_{\ast}}(\rho+w_{\ast})\frac{e^{2\rho+2w_{\ast}}(1-\Psi(\rho))e^{-\delta_{0}\langle\alpha\rangle^{1/2}}}{|k|\big|B(\rho+w_{\ast})-B(w_{\ast})+i\iota\epsilon\big|}\big|A_{k}\big[h^{1}_{0k}(\rho,\cdot)e^{i\alpha\cdot}\big](w)\big|\,d\rho d\alpha\\
&\qquad+\varrho_{\varkappa_{k},w_{\ast}}(w_{\ast})\Big\|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\langle k,\eta\rangle^{-2}e^{-\delta_{0}\langle\eta+\alpha\rangle^{1/2}}\Big|\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{i\alpha\rho}A_{k}\big[h_{0k}^{1\ast}(\rho,w)\big](w)\,d\rho\Big|d\alpha\Big\|_{L^{2}(\eta\in\mathbb{R})}.\end{split}

(7.55)

In view of (
7.25
)-(
7.26
), from (
7.55
) we conclude that
